Scientists Warn: The World Has Less Than Four Years of Carbon Budget Left

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Science  |  Read Source →

Solar power, wind energy, and electric vehicles are reshaping global emissions trends, but a new scientific assessment reveals that the world’s remaining carbon budget is shrinking at an alarming rate.

Global fossil fuel emissions are expected to fall slightly in 2026, but the world’s total carbon dioxide output is still projected to rise. A new assessment by the Global Carbon Project estimates that fossil CO2 emissions will decline by 0.4% to 38.2 billion metric tons (42.1 billion U.S. tons), partly because of expanding clean energy and disruptions linked to the Iran conflict.

However, increasing emissions from deforestation and other changes in land use are expected to push total global CO2 emissions to 43.3 billion metric tons (47.7 billion U.S. tons), up from 42.8 billion metric tons in 2025. The findings show that renewable energy is beginning to reshape global emissions trends, but its growth has not yet produced a sustained worldwide decline.

Oil is expected to account for much of the projected reduction in fossil fuel emissions. Global CO2 emissions from oil are forecast to drop by 1.9%, largely reflecting the energy shock associated with the Iran conflict, which has increased prices and constrained supplies. Natural gas emissions are expected to decrease by 0.3%, while coal emissions could rise by 0.6%. Emissions from international aviation and shipping are each projected to fall by 1%, with reduced activity in the Middle East contributing to the aviation decline.

“A decline in fossil CO2 emissions is encouraging, but a reduction driven partly by geopolitical shocks is not a sustainable climate solution,” said Professor Pierre Friedlingstein, director of the Global Carbon Budget Office at the University of Exeter, who led the study. “The long-term answer remains the rapid deployment of clean energy technologies. While renewables and electric vehicles are increasingly offsetting growth in energy demand, further acceleration is needed if global emissions are to peak and enter a sustained decline.”

Glen Peters, Research Professor at the CICERO Center for International Climate Research, emphasized that temporary reductions should not be confused with lasting progress. “A drop in emissions in one year is not a peak unless it is driven by structural change. Accelerated deployment of solar, wind, batteries, and electric vehicles are pushing emissions down in dozens of countries, and slowing emissions growth in many more, but the changes are not sufficient to lock in a peak in global emissions.”

Solar and wind power, together with electric vehicles, are increasingly limiting emissions despite rising energy demand. According to the assessment, these technologies are meeting or avoiding roughly two-thirds of the increase in global energy demand. Solar and wind generation alone are projected to prevent 4.6 billion metric tons (5.1 billion U.S. tons) of CO2 emissions in 2026, compared with 4.1 billion metric tons in 2025 and just 0.28 billion metric tons in 2010.

China is central to this transformation. Although it produces approximately 32% of global fossil fuel emissions, it also accounts for half of the emissions avoided worldwide through renewable energy and electric vehicles. China’s emissions are projected to decline by 0.9% in 2026, helped by lower oil consumption and continued renewable electricity expansion. Coal emissions, however, are expected to increase slightly by 0.3%. China also leads the world in carbon uptake from reforestation and afforestation (new forests).

“Clean technologies are making a real difference in reducing global emissions. Solar and wind power and electric vehicles are expanding rapidly, led by China. Countries on every continent are decreasing their emissions while growing their economies. A further acceleration could turn the tide on global emissions within a few years,” said Professor Corinne Le Quéré, Royal Society Research Professor at the University of East Anglia’s School of Environmental Sciences.

Emissions are projected to decline by 1.3% in the United States, primarily because coal consumption is falling after increasing in 2025 amid high natural gas prices and cold weather. The European Union is expected to record a 1.5% reduction, while Japan’s emissions could decrease by 1%. India is moving in the opposite direction, with emissions projected to rise by 4.4%, exceeding its average annual growth rate of 3.5% over the past decade. Emissions across the rest of the world are expected to decrease by 0.7%, largely because of reduced oil consumption.

An accompanying analysis, currently in press in a separate peer-reviewed journal, identifies a broader shift in the relationship between economic growth and carbon emissions. In 59 countries, emissions were lower in 2025 than in 2019, before the COVID-19 pandemic, even as their economies expanded. Emissions growth has also slowed in regions where total emissions continue to rise, including China, India, and other emerging economies.

The decline in fossil fuel emissions is being offset by increasing carbon releases from land-use change (such as deforestation). These emissions are projected to reach 5.1 billion metric tons (5.6 billion U.S. tons) in 2026. The increase is linked partly to peat fires and forest degradation in Southeast Asia, where drier conditions associated with El Niño have heightened fire risks. Nearly all peat fires are ignited by human activities.

Natural ecosystems also influence how much CO2 remains in the atmosphere. Forests, soils, and oceans absorb substantial amounts of carbon, acting as natural carbon sinks. The Global Carbon Budget reports that land-based carbon absorption has recovered after weakening sharply during the 2023 and 2024 El Niño event. However, the powerful El Niño developing in 2026 is expected to affect this natural carbon uptake over the coming year.

The latest assessment estimates that only 150 billion metric tons (165 billion U.S. tons) of CO2 can be emitted before cumulative emissions reach the level associated with 1.5°C (2.7°F) of global warming above preindustrial levels. At current emission rates, that remaining carbon budget amounts to less than four years. Meanwhile, atmospheric CO2 concentrations are projected to reach 427.7 parts per million in 2026, approximately 54% above preindustrial levels.

Removing carbon dioxide from the atmosphere is another part of the challenge. Permanent deforestation released an average of approximately 3.8 billion metric tons (4.2 billion U.S. tons) of CO2 annually between 2016 and 2025. Reforestation and afforestation offset roughly half of those emissions. China, the European Union, and the United States account for the largest combined removals through forest restoration and expansion, while deforestation outweighs these gains in countries including Brazil, Russia, and Indonesia. Technologies designed to remove atmospheric CO2 remain limited, currently capturing less than the amount released by fossil fuels in just 30 minutes.

“Alongside reducing emissions, we need to scale up CO2 removal to reach greenhouse gas neutrality. Afforestation and reforestation already remove substantial amounts of CO2, but these gains are still more than offset by emissions from deforestation, and novel carbon removal remains very limited. We need to act on all these fronts now,” said Professor Julia Pongratz of Ludwig-Maximilian-University Munich.

Despite continued increases in total emissions, their long-term growth rate has slowed substantially. Global CO2 emissions rose by an average of 0.3% annually over the past decade, compared with 1.7% annually during the preceding decade. The accompanying analysis projects that between now and the end of 2028, additional solar and wind generation and electric vehicles could prevent emissions equivalent to 64% to 79% of those associated with rising energy demand. Electric vehicles are becoming increasingly influential, although only about 10% of the global passenger car fleet was electrified in 2025 (or 6% excluding hybrids).

The researchers conclude that continued deployment of clean technologies could bring the first sustained global decline in CO2 emissions within reach. However, achieving that outcome depends on maintaining and accelerating their expansion rather than relying on temporary reductions caused by economic or geopolitical disruptions. As the authors write, “If further acceleration of clean technology deployment can be supported through time, a sustained peak and decline in global CO2 emissions could be a few years away.”

A former Premier League scout believes Leeds United should set their sights on Middlesbrough marksman Will Lankshear.

The 21-year-old only joined Middlesbrough in the summer, sealing a move from Tottenham Hotspur for an initial fee reported to be in the £10m region.

He has made a stunning start to life at the Riverside, notching 15 goals across his first 16 appearances in the club’s colours.

His scinillating form has fuelled talk of a potential England call-up - despite the fact he is playing in the second tier.

Mick Brown, a former scout for the likes of Manchester United and Blackburn Rovers, believes Leeds should be among those on his trail.

He told Football Insider: “Will Lankshear is obviously going to have a lot of Premier League clubs looking at him. He's proving already he's a top-class striker, it's not like he's just come out of nowehere, he's somebody these clubs have been aware of even when he was at Tottenham.

Middlesbrough will be determined to hold on to Lankshear, especially considering they are firmly in the Championship promotion picture.

However, the lure of the Premier League often proves strong for the second tier’s leading lights. Leeds sit ninth in the Premier League table and currently have Dominic Calvert-Lewin and Lukas Nmecha as their forward options.

Rocket Report: Starship set for historic test flight; a century of Electron

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

“You can simplify it as a big tank feeding engines.”

Welcome to Edition 9.19 of the Rocket Report! Will something historic happen this weekend? No orbital rocket has ever been fully recovered with the intent of reusing all of its components. But that’s just what SpaceX is going to try to do with a Starship launch on Friday, and an upper stage landing attempt 22.5 hours later on Saturday afternoon, local time. Ars will be on hand in Starbase, Texas, to provide coverage of it all.

Electron set for milestone launch. The next launch of Rocket Lab’s Electron vehicle is due to lift off from the Mahia Peninsula in New Zealand on November 14 at 5:30 UTC. This mission will serve as Electron’s 100th overall mission—a notable milestone for the small rocket that first flew in 2017, NASASpaceflight.com reports.

A true commercial success … This flight of Electron, named “The Mountain God Rises,” will deliver another iQPS satellite into LEO for iQPS’s synthetic aperture radar constellation. The anniversary rocket features a special gold-and-black livery which looks fantastic. Rocket Lab truly does deserve recognition as the world’s second purely commercial launch business to make a successful go at it. We are all celebrating with them.

Is the Vega rocket a dead end? Last month, Italian rocket builder Avio presented a market update that outlined the additional investment required to increase the annual launch cadence of its Vega C rocket to six and eventually nine flights per year, European Spaceflight reports. The need for this additional funding raises an interesting question about the future of Italy’s Vega launch vehicle family. Is investing in the infrastructure needed to secure just three more Vega C flights per year by the end of the decade the best use of resources, particularly when several emerging European launch companies appear poised to offer comparable capacity?

Boosters may be better used by Ariane 6 … Vega C isn’t going anywhere. But it’s an inescapable reality that a single Ariane 64 launch requires the equivalent of an entire year’s worth of solid rocket boosters for Vega C at its baseline annual launch cadence of four flights. Does it make sense to continue investing in expanding Vega C’s launch cadence and later introducing Vega E when the future of both vehicles depends on production capacity that is increasingly demanded by Ariane 6?

Synspective inks 10 Alpha rockets. Firefly Aerospace announced on Tuesday a multi-launch agreement with Synspective, a Japanese synthetic aperture radar satellite data and solutions provider, for up to 10 launches on Firefly’s Alpha rocket through 2030. With the ability to launch 1,000 kg to low Earth orbit, Alpha will deliver two of Synspective’s SAR satellites to orbit per mission, deploying up to 20 total satellites.

Getting it done with one ton … Under the agreement, Firefly will launch Synspective’s SAR satellites from both Pad-0A at Wallops Flight Facility in Virginia and Space Launch Complex 2 at Vandenberg Space Force Base in California. “This landmark agreement reflects growing customer confidence in our Alpha Block II vehicle,” said Jason Kim, CEO of Firefly Aerospace. “With 1-ton payload capacity and responsive launch capabilities, Alpha is a natural fit for customers like Synspective seeking direct access to orbit to get their operations up and running sooner.”

Stoke and Planet sign multi-launch agreement. Stoke Space on Monday announced a multi-launch agreement with Planet Labs PBC. The companies did not announce the terms of the agreement, including the total number of launches under contract, nor the cost of the launches. “We need launch partners who move as quickly as we do,” said Will Marshall, Co-Founder & CEO of Planet.

An open-ended agreement? … The announcement suggests this could be an open-ended agreement, however. “Planet builds and operates the largest fleet of Earth-imaging satellites in orbit and captures imagery of Earth’s landmass every day,” a related release says. “Operators like Planet launch on a regular basis to expand and replenish their fleets, and that steady demand is what our Nova fleet is designed to serve.”

The launch crunch comes to national security. The US military is taking a new approach to prop up commercial launch companies as SpaceX looks to wind down its Falcon rocket program, suggesting defense officials see the workhorse rocket’s retirement as a potential national security risk, Ars reports. The Pentagon’s Office of Strategic Capital has twice in the last two weeks announced it was sending cash to two of SpaceX’s competitors in the launch business.

More pads and rockets … First, on October 26, the office announced a $150 million conditional loan commitment to Stoke Space. Military officials revealed a second $150 million Pentagon loan Tuesday for Rocket Lab. Stoke Space’s loan is tailored to support the buildout of the company’s second launch pad at Cape Canaveral Space Force Station, Florida. The loan to Rocket Lab will support the company’s efforts to increase the flight rate of its partially reusable Neutron rocket.

Rocket Lab raises Neutron price. Citing strong customer demand and a constrained market for launch, Rocket Lab announced this week it had increased the cost of a dedicated Neutron rocket from $55 million to $70 million, Payload reports. In recent months, the coming shortage of launch capacity in the US—mainly due to SpaceX sunsetting its Transporter rideshare program—has sparked a flurry of interest in new medium-lift rocket entrants.

Positive on Neutron … “Neutron is rapidly establishing itself as the backbone of next-generation constellation deployment and government missions in the medium-lift launch class,” CEO Peter Beck said in a statement. “Strong international demand and a growing launch manifest, at a new price point, is proof that Neutron is the launch vehicle the market has been waiting for.” The price hike for Neutron isn’t keeping customers away as new bookings continue, the company said. Customers’ next options to fly on Neutron are now in the 2030s and beyond.

Rockets are not hard? During an hour-long event on Thursday, Abel Avellan, the founder and chief executive of the telecommunications company AST SpaceMobile, laid out his vision for developing a large rocket to launch his Bluebird satellites into orbit, Ars reports. Avellan emphasized, over and over again, that AST plans to build a “simple” rocket. He paraphrased SpaceX founder Elon Musk several times, saying, “the best part is no part.”

This sounds a little naive … “Of course rocket engineers are going to get mad with me, but you can simplify it as a big tank feeding engines,” he said of the rocket. “So big tanks—we know how to do them. We know how to do them. We know how to do them in composite.” The Blacklift rocket will be “simple,” he said, because it will be solely tailored to lift five Bluebird satellites into low-Earth orbit at a time. It will have 20 metric tons of lift capacity, a reusable first stage, and launch twice a month from a pad at Wallops Flight Facility in Virginia that formerly served the Antares rocket. (submitted by thegooglemonster)

When will the Eclipse rocket be ready? During an earnings call this week (see transcript), Firefly CEO Kim spoke about the company’s ongoing work to develop the Eclipse rocket. “We’re heads down on Eclipse,” he said. “We’re laser-focused on delivering the first stage to our co-developer Northrop Grumman. And we’re over 175 hot fires on Miranda, counting the one from today. And we’re looking at all the data, verifying the capability. It’s a very mature engine, and we’re very confident in its capabilities.”

Probably not launching soon … However, when an analyst pressed on Firefly getting a launch in 2027, Kim did not offer a direct answer. “Everything is just laser-focused on all the milestones in terms of qualifying Miranda and qualifying our composite tanks and fluid systems as well,” he said. “So we’re really, really just excited about the future of Eclipse, especially with this injection of capital that will help accelerate our production line.” Bottom line: Don’t expect to see Eclipse on a launch pad any time soon.

Starship set for 15th test flight on Friday. Flight 15 of Starship is preparing to launch as soon as Friday, November 13, pending regulatory approval, SpaceX said. The 75-minute launch window will open at 4:45 p.m. CT (22:45 UTC). The upcoming flight is planned to be the first to return both Starship and Super Heavy to the launch site for catch. If successful, this will mark the first full recovery of an orbital launch system.

All eyes on the ship … Starship’s initial return-to-launch-site mission is expected to orbit the Earth at an altitude of about 275 km and complete approximately 14 orbits around the planet over the course of a more than 22-hour flight. Starship’s primary mission will be to deliver 26 Starlink V3 satellites to orbit. But really, this mission is all about the landings, first of the booster and then, more dramatically, of the upper stage Starship on Saturday.

SpaceX is now an ‘infrastructure’ company. For a long time, SpaceX has been synonymous with developing newer, larger, and ever-more-reusable rockets, Ars reports. But that began to change over the last decade with the introduction of Starlink, and the change has accelerated during the last 18 months as the company has pivoted to data and artificial intelligence. At last week’s UP.Summit conference in Florida, SpaceX’s vice president of launch, Kiko Dontchev, offered some of the most revealing comments to date about the company’s changing focus.

From AI to Mars? … “In the last couple years, when I talk about SpaceX, yes, we are a rocket company, but fundamentally we are an infrastructure company at this point,” Dontchev said. “We’re building infrastructure on the ground and low Earth orbit, soon on the Moon, and obviously eventually Mars.” However, Dontchev insisted that human settlement and Mars remain part of the core of SpaceX’s aspirations. “We have come to understand that this tool is critical to us accelerating our pace of innovation,” he said of artificial intelligence. “Without AI, I think we still would have gotten to the Moon. I think we still get to Mars with SI. We’re going to get there so much faster.”

Nov. 13: Vulcan | Amazon Leo LV-01 | Cape Canaveral Space Force Station, Florida | 17:54 UTC

Nov. 13: Starship | Flight 15 | Starbase, Texas | 22:45 UTC

Nov. 14: Soyuz 2.1b | MKA Kinosputnik, others | Vostochny Cosmodrome, Russia | 00:50 UTC

With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Jeffries shredded over Hegseth impeachment snafu as Dem jockeys for speaker’s gavel

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Back-to-back blunders on the House floor are raising questions over whether Democrats are ready for primetime as they're poised to take over the majority — and Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries, D-N.Y., makes a bid for the top role.

Democrats are projected to win 255 House seats, with 12 races yet to be called, and Jeffries is running to be Speaker of the House following the sweeping victories last week that saw the party make gains even in traditional Republican strongholds like Texas, Mississippi, Kansas and South Carolina.

But some have questioned whether the recent snafus signal that Jeffries and other leaders may not be ready to be in command of the House majority.

"I may have to rethink my support of Hakeem Jeffries after the Hegseth impeachment vote," Fred Trump III, a Democrat, author and nephew of President Donald Trump, posted on X. "Eleven Democrats do not show up? All Republicans vote it down."

"Pete Hegseth is laughing at you and is stronger today. Shame," he continued. "A slap in the face of the military and veterans on this important day."

House Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries speaks alongside Rep. Pete Aguilar and Rep. Katherine Clark during a news conference in Washington, D.C., on Jan. 8, 2026. (Anna Moneymaker/Getty Images)

HEGSETH THWARTS GOP-LED IMPEACHMENT PUSH AS QUESTIONS SWIRL OVER WHETHER ALLEGATIONS RISE TO HIGH CRIMES

House GOP leaders successfully blocked Rep. Thomas Massie, R-Ky., from leading the charge to impeach Secretary of War Pete Hegseth earlier this week.

Part of the reason for the failure was the absence of 11 House Democrats during a procedural vote on Tuesday evening.

Had just seven of them been present, the vote to kill Massie's impeachment resolution would have failed, triggering debate and a vote on impeaching the Cabinet secretary likely the next day.

"Everyone knew that attendance was key tonight but somehow Democratic leaders failed to deliver and handed Republicans a win," Demand Progress Executive Director Sean Vitka said in a statement.

'THE VIEW' CO-HOST CALLS DEMOCRATS MISSING HEGSETH IMPEACHMENT VOTE A 'SELF-INFLICTED WOUND'

Progressive journalist David Schuster wrote on X, "Sure, house Dems will have 259 seats in January instead of current 213. And Hegseth impeachment can be reintroduced. But Hakeem having 11 Dem absences today on Hegseth (thus losing vote) reveals a Hakeem leadership problem. Embarrassing."

An emotional Rep. Melanie Stansbury, D-N.M., said in a video posted to X that she and other Democrats were stuck in traffic when they missed the vote.

Various reports, however, said they were at a fundraiser less than two miles from the U.S. Capitol.

U.S. Secretary of War Pete Hegseth takes questions from reporters during a news conference at the Pentagon on Nov. 10, 2026, in Arlington, Va. (Chip Somodevilla/Getty Images)

HOUSE DEM MISSES HEGSETH IMPEACHMENT VOTE BECAUSE OF MAJOR MEDICAL PROCEDURE ONE WEEK AFTER ELECTION DAY

Others blamed medical procedures and family emergencies.

The day after, a bill Democratic leadership was actively urging lawmakers to vote against passed by unanimous voice vote — simply because no Democrats called for a roll call instead.

Democrats accused Republicans of parliamentary tricks, including a post on the X account belonging to the House Veterans Affairs Committee minority, "Republicans pushed it through that way intentionally so their members wouldn’t have to go on the record supporting cuts to veterans’ benefits."

But the majority of Democrats do not believe it is an early blight on Jeffries' potential speakership.

HAKEEM JEFFRIES DECLARES BID FOR HOUSE SPEAKER AFTER GOP'S MIDTERM MASSACRE

"I think there's frustration in terms of whipping votes. I'm not entirely sure where the breakdown is.  And it does seem like there's maybe some tension somewhere," Democratic strategist Mike Nellis told Fox News Digital. "I think it'll be incumbent on the incoming speaker to make sure that everybody feels like they're on the same page ... beyond that, if you're asking me if it makes a difference in a leadership flight, I mean, nobody's running against Hakeem. I haven't heard of anybody seriously thinking about doing that, so I don't know that it'll change anything."

He said House Democrats largely "have faith" in Jeffries and House Minority Whip Katherine Clark, D-Mass., who is poised to be the next House Majority Leader.

Democrat Dan Turrentine, co-host of "The Huddle," told Fox News Digital "a few Democrats unintentionally missed one vote. Unfortunately, this happens periodically in both chambers, for both parties. It does not speak to Jeffries' leadership."

And other House Democrats, when asked, directed frustration at their colleagues instead.

Rep. Melanie Stansbury, D-N.M., arrives for the House Oversight and Government Reform Committee deposition of former Attorney General Pam Bondi about the Jeffrey Epstein files, in the Rayburn building on Friday, May 29, 2026. (Tom Williams/CQ-Roll Call, Inc via Getty Images)

"My message is always to members that we need to be here and be on time," Clark told reporters Wednesday. "But there was a clear message from the Republican Party as well that they are unwilling, even after this election, to stand up against Hegseth in this war."

Rep. Jared Moskowitz, D-Fla., said, "It's unfortunate, but that's not on leadership."

4 Hours Saved, 20 Hours In The Air: Will Passengers Choose Qantas’ Project Sunrise?

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Well over 20 hours on a single flight sounds like hell to a lot of passengers. But enduring this will be necessary for those wishing to use Qantas Project Sunrise flights, which are set to begin in 2027 with services from Sydney Kingsford-Smith Airport (SYD) to London Heathrow Airport (LHR).

Qantas will also demand a premium, arguing that passengers can expect to save around four hours and avoid the hassle of an international transfer. Despite the clear difficulties that Project Sunrise could pose, particularly for economy class passengers, Qantas is banking on its success.

Airways Magazine reports that the airline expects to add AU$400 million ($274 million) in earnings before interest and tax through the services. High demand will be vital to secure such positive results, particularly given that challenges such as high fuel costs, a weakened Australian dollar and higher labor and airport costs have emerged since 2022.

Project Sunrise's opening route will be between SYD and LHR, a mammoth route of approximately 9,188 nautical miles (17,000 km). Traveling from Britain to Australia currently usually requires a change, which is often in Singapore or the Middle East. Such stopovers make the route longer, and passengers also have to waste time on the ground.

It is expected that Project Sunrise flights will take around 21 hours and 40 minutes. This will save approximately four hours. In return for the time savings and the unique nature of this flight, Qantas expects to receive a 20% higher Revenue per Available Seat Kilometer (RASK) compared with comparable one-stop itineraries.

When it arrives in 2027, Project Sunrise will overtake Singapore Airlines Airbus A350-900ULR flights as the world's longest. That will secure Qantas' position as the leading premium airline in Australia and a key player among the airlines that offer ultra-long-haul flying. However, regardless of the added convenience, some passengers will struggle with being on an aircraft for over 20 hours, while others will find the higher price off-putting.

41% of seats will be in premium cabins, versus 30% in premium cabins for Qantas' Boeing 787-9 and Airbus A380, Airways Mag reports. These seats are divided between first, business, and premium economy class. Such a high number of premium seats takes inspiration from Singapore Airlines' A350-900ULRs, which are exclusively fitted with premium economy and business class seats.

It's not surprising that Qantas expects these products to be popular, given that a flight of over 20 hours will give passengers the opportunity to take advantage of the luxury. The table below uses Qantas' data to show the key specifications for the A350-1000ULR. It covers both the aircraft itself and the four cabins onboard.

9,700-10,000 nautical miles (18,000-18,520 km)

The ultimate in luxury comes from first class, a product that Singapore Airlines has skipped on its A350-900ULR. These six suites are distinguished from business class by a separate armchair and a true flat bed, rather than one that reclines. This class also has a huge entertainment screen, along with a flexible space for eating and working with up to two guests.

Behind first class is an enormous 52-seat business class cabin. The suite has a privacy door and totals 42 inches (100 cm) wide. At 80 inches (2 m), the flat bed is one inch (2.5 cm) longer than what Qantas offers on its Airbus A380 business class product. All but the very tallest of guests should find this roomy enough. Finally, customers can choose from within a 40-seat premium economy cabin.

These seats are in a 2-4-2 configuration and have 40 inches (100 cm) of pitch. Australian designer David Caon says that “the Premium Economy cabin has been redesigned from the ground up with a focus on ergonomics, entertainment and privacy.” The new headrest wings are the biggest visible difference and will provide passengers with both additional support and a sense of privacy without isolating them from travel companions.

The main reason that Qantas could fail is an inability to sell economy class seats. Many passengers on a budget would rather take the hit of a longer total travel time for the chance to stretch their legs during a one-stop itinerary. Before the ongoing crisis in the Middle East, it was also popular to use the stop as an excuse to enjoy a short vacation in the region, such as in Dubai, before the main trip to Australia or the United Kingdom.

Qantas hopes it can sway wary passengers through a high-quality economy class product. The cabin has 140 seats, with 42 in Economy Plus. These seats are in a 3-3-3 configuration. A relatively generous 33 inches (83.8 cm) of pitch was initially promised, although Qantas has now backtracked to saying the pitch will vary in different parts of the aircraft.

22 inches (56 cm) in the separate reclining armchair

A six-way adjustable headrest gives a greater degree of customization compared to what you can usually expect in economy class. Two fast-charging outlets per seat also means that your entertainment won't run out. Economy Plus will be broadly the same but with priority boarding and access to overhead lockers, as well as a little bit more legroom. The table above uses Qantas' data to compare the seat dimensions that you can enjoy, or suffer in, across each class.

However, initial predictions are that Qantas will attract strong demand for its economy class seats aboard Project Sunrise, partly because it has intentionally produced a small economy class cabin. Executive Traveller's case study predicts a load factor of 85%. This is lower than what is expected in the premium classes, but still more than enough to sustain the service.

Regardless of the quality of the economy class, you would still be spending over 20 hours in a seat designed for affordability rather than comfort. Some airlines that fly ultra-long-haul routes are getting creative, such as United Airlines' Relax Row, which makes lying flat in economy class possible.

The Australian carrier's answer to make such a long time in economy class possible is its Wellbeing Zone, which is available to both economy and premium economy class passengers. Qantas calls it "an on board destination to promote movement, stretching and hydration throughout your flight."

Within the Wellbeing Zone, you'll find a selection of beverages and refreshments available through self-service. The area will also have monitors to guide you through movements, ensuring you can stay mobile during the flight. "Sculpted surfaces and integrated handles" will also facilitate stretching. Qantas Group Chief Executive Officer Alan Joyce said that:

“Fewer seats translate to more space for each customer and a dedicated Wellbeing Zone for travellers to stretch, help themselves to a snack and spend time out of their seat. We are the only airline in the world that will have a bespoke designed onboard stretch and movement space,".

Airbus' A350-1000ULR begins testing as Project Sunrise moves closer to reality.

Qantas has made a vast investment to order 12 bespoke A350-1000ULR aircraft that will carry out the services. However, it is also investing time and personnel to get Project Sunrise off the ground. It will train over 360 pilots and 1,200 cabin crew, or 30 pilots and 100 cabin crew per aircraft, in the unique skills required for these services.

Such a substantial training operation is needed because of regulatory arrangements Qantas has made with the Civil Aviation Safety Authority, requiring far more crew members aboard every Project Sunrise flight. The Project Sunrise pilot training is occurring at a simulator in Sydney.

It is Australia's first A350 simulator, which was installed ahead of the first A350-1000ULR's arrival. The first cohort has been drawn from Qantas' current A330 pilots, with 40 involved in the first wave. That decision was made because of numerous commonalities between the A350 and the A330.

It's a race against time to get the crews ready for the expected first A350-1000ULR delivery in April 2027. It is currently expected that this first aircraft will fly to New Zealand to take training out of the simulator and familiarize the crew with its operations. The jet will then be pressed into regular service to the United Kingdom and, later, the US.

Billy Sharp sends EFL Championship promotion warning to Birmingham City

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

EFL legend and all-time Championship top scorer Billy Sharp has warned Birmingham City that there's still "a long way to go" after Chris Davies' side reached the second-tier summit ahead of the ongoing international break.

The Blues enjoyed an exceptional run of games between the three-week break in September and October and this current one, winning six of their seven games, three by at least a three-goal margin.

They dropped points only at Cardiff City, a side that has yet to lose at home this season, and have maintained their own unbeaten home record, winning all four games to add some gloss to a St Andrew's record which was pretty draw-heavy in the infancy of the campaign.

The win against former table-toppers Swansea City saw them climb above Middlesbrough on goal difference, and they will hold that top spot for at least another week until action resumes ahead of the Christmas period.

The question in the meantime, though, is: Can they stay there?

Billy Sharp has been a regular pundit on EFL shows this season, after formally announcing his retirement from the professional game back in October. The 40-year-old was promoted out of the Championship three times in his career, so he knows what it takes to maintain top-two form.

The all-time Championship top scorer waxed lyrical over Birmingham through the opening 15 games of the campaign, and was actually surprised that their run to the top of the table hasn't been given as much attention as it arguably should have.

FLW looks at which of the division's 24 clubs are the biggest and lowest spenders

"I think you saw glimpses last season, but they obviously didn't have that consistency," he told the Essential EFL Podcast. "They've done it really quietly, actually; no one's really spoken about them.

"He’s got the two settled centre-halves, which I've liked since last season, and I think he's playing a 4-2-3-1, which really suits him at the minute.

"He seems to have a settled side and had a little sprinkle with Millar coming in, adding a bit of magic down the left, and Stansfield got the two goals, which should do him a world of good. We know he’s got the ability, and when he’s firing, he can win games for Birmingham."

It's so far, so good for the Blues this season, but we are just 15 games in. With over two-thirds of the year still to go, and just one point separating Chris Davies' side from West Brom in fifth, Sharp feels that they can't rest on their laurels at all.

"Am I surprised they're top? I am," he admitted. "But we were saying this about Swansea on the last international break.

"It’s still tight at the top, fair play to Birmingham; going into this international break they are top, but there’s still a long way to go."

As Sharp mentioned, Birmingham's current run of six wins in seven to reach the top of the Championship has gone slightly under the radar, with the main second-tier narratives surrounding West Ham United's drop-off, or Middlesbrough struggling without Will Lankshear, for example.

Now, though, this Blues side are at the forefront of the conversation, and will be until league action resumes after the international break. Chris Davies' side are there to be shot at, and all eyes will be on whether they can handle the weight of expectation.

It's arguable that they didn't last season, with many expecting them to follow in the footsteps of Ipswich Town and make it back-to-back promotions after their record-breaking 111-point campaign in League One the year prior.

Pick any two players. Send the code, and your mate enters it here to play the same pair.

But this year, the squad is much more settled. Partnerships which were forming last year are fixed now, and there hasn't been as much squad churn as there was last summer at St Andrew's.

Plus, Chris Davies' decision to move away from a patient, heavy possession-based style has worked wonders, with the Blues packing a real punch in an attacking sense due to their desire to get the ball forward quicker.

They look a lot more ready to maintain results and handle pressure as they become the side teams want to knock off the pedestal.

Sharp also spoke about how this exact conversation was being made around Swansea's chances at promotion, with Vitor Matos' side sitting atop the Championship at the last international break.

And while the Swans did return to action with back-to-back wins, they followed that up with four defeats in five leading up to the break, including that loss at St Andrew's last time out.

They're still in contention, sitting three points off Birmingham, but the difference in performances with the pressure on, compared to when it's not, was there to be seen, and Blues have to guard against the same happening to them.

Do you think Birmingham City can maintain this promotion-winning form? Let us know in the comments.

Chris Davies' side have a tough matchup at Stoke City when play resumes, which then leads them into a run of four games against sides sitting between 18th and 22nd. A win at the bet365 Stadium would propel them into a run of, on paper, easier matches.

Perhaps, by mid-December, this could be a side with some daylight between themselves and the chasing pack, and then people may be taking their current title charge a lot more seriously.

Pennsylvania Pebbles

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It hasn’t rained for weeks. That changes today, just in time to join forces with the Autumn leaves and northern Pennsylvania rocks to brew up a wicked hiker test.

They can’t help themselves, these white-tailed deer. They like to visit us campers. At 3:00AM this morning a deer, which had almost certainly sensed the rain coming, perhaps curious if there was space inside for it to keep dry. I told it that to save about $50 I bought a 1 person tent, it muttered a snort before prancing off.

The next thing I remember rain was pattering onto my tent. Shucks, I wasn’t going to escape with a dry tent this morning. I had been clever enough to tuck my groundsheet behind the rain fly so that the water would fall to the ground rather than pool up. The fact that I was camping on a layer of leaves was a boon as well, I wouldn’t have to deal with mud getting on everything. I fell back asleep but emerged from my tent shortly before sunrise to an eerie scene – the forest was enveloped in a thick mist. My tent looked sorry, the water weighing down the rain fly, the stakes no doubt shifting from their precarious posts during the night. Usually this is the time where I come up with some excuse to stay in my tent for half the morning, but not this time. The rain had brought about 10 degrees of warmth along with it, it was in the mid fifties. It wasn’t currently raining, so I certainly didn’t want to dilly dally only for it to start raining, making packing up a mess. I wolfed down my breakfast and set about packing. By 7:00AM I was making my way northbound.

I had made it a grand total on 1 mile north of the Leroy A. Smith shelter yesterday, leaving a little over 18 miles back to my van at the Delaware Water Gap. I knew rain could arrive at any time, there was a decent chance of it throughout the day. I was liking the foggy trail conditions, though it came at the expense of the valley views I had yesterday.

On the north side of Wind Gap a rocky yet mercifully short climb awaited me. Once the trail leveled out, I found that the area was still consumed with fog. As I continued my meandering, a small flock of birds, perhaps starlings, took off from the ground near the trail and disappeared into the white mist. Several chipmunks scampered into their burrows, revealing little more than a flash of brown fur to me. My thoughts ran to the interaction between these two very different species of animals. Where are the birds searching for food, potentially at the expense of the chipmunks? Had I stumbled upon nature’s version of capture the flag?

The scene on the trail early this morning.

I wouldn’t watch a scary movie before wandering into this part of the forest.

For a while the rain held off. I enjoyed more of the trail surrounded by fog from all sides. It creates some fascinating scenes for sure. I didn’t miss the valley views, I had something completely different to experience. Nature was about to throw something different to experience at me. Right after I turned onto the Wolf Rocks bypass trail, the mist that had been on and off all day turned into a light rain. I tossed on my poncho for the first time since Vermont. I had made excellent progress, most of the bad rocky sections were behind me. I trudged on through the fog and rain determined to stay on track for an early finish.

Suddenly I wasn’t alone, ahead of me a figure was descending the trail. On a day like this, it could only be a long distance hiker. A large yellow pack and sky blue sun hoodie confirmed as much. Then I realized that I knew those items and the face of the hiker well, Pebbles! I called out to her, yet again struck by the encounter as I was sure that this time she would have been far down the trail. In fact, she had spent Halloween trick-or-treating back in Unionville, NY. That explained why I only saw her once last week, jumping ahead on the trail should have put us on track to meet again the next day. She reported the town was very friendly to her and her thru-hiker costume. She may be a genius, I kicked myself for not thinking of the idea. Maybe I’ll buy some candy for myself at the post-halloween discount.

Pebbles and See You Tomorrow catch up on the trail.

I didn’t envy Pebbles, who was heading straight into the rocky sections that tested me yesterday. I, on the other hand, reached the gravel road near Mount Minsi. I could really make time on the wide open path. After a brief rocky descent on the AT I switched back onto the gravel road. I figured it would be a much smoother descent, and it also led straight back to the AT just before the trailhead where my van was parked. It was not perfect, there were plenty of rocks on the primitive road, but there were none of the step downs that I would have had on the AT. My ankle has taken a lot of abuse this year, but it feels up to finishing off the hike and I want to keep it that way. Just before the trailhead, I noticed and abrupt change in the color of the gravel on the road. It turned into a mix of turquoise and gray, a combination I felt was worth admiring for a few moments before finishing out my hike.

I was back at my van around 1:30PM and quickly got myself ready for town. The local chain cafe was open until 10:00PM giving me time to get all 4 blog posts from this week ready for publishing. With luck I could have another light Sunday coming up, just what I need as I enter the closing stages of my year long hike!

Yes, I caught myself admiring the rocks in northern Pennsylvania of all places!

The buyer pays the same price as they would otherwise, and your purchase helps to support The Trek's ongoing goal to serve you quality backpacking advice and information. Thanks for your support!

To learn more, please visit the About This Site page.

I’m See You Tomorrow (SYT), a 30-something hiker who loves hiking just for the thrill of seeing things that most people don’t. I have no problem eating the same things every day and, for me, over-analyzing everything is half the fun (sometimes more). I am new to backpacking but that won’t stop me from taking on an unorthodox hike of the Appalachian Trail in 2026. It’s not quite a yo-yo, but I have aspirations of hiking the entire trail twice in one year. For the rest of the story you’ll just have to start reading my blog!

9 Bedroom Makeover Tips for More Sex

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

And we’re starting with things you already have.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Your partner carries you into the bedroom à la your favorite #BookTok MMC, only for you both to remember the duvet is still covered with laundry that needs to be folded. Buzzkill. Or maybe you reach into your nightstand drawer for your wand vibrator, only to graze a stack of unpaid bills. Never mind…

“Your bedroom is already shaping how you feel about sex,” says Ashley Giddens, interior designer, artist and founder of Ashthetics NYC. It's not just clutter that can pull you out of the moment, or a tidy space that can help you stay in it, either, she says.

“Light impacts how you feel, which matters a lot when you're about to take your clothes off, while the textures impact what you feel beyond skin,” Giddens explains. Not to mention, the colors on the walls, sheets, and even the ceiling can influence how cozy, intimate, or downright sexy your bedroom feels, she says.

With that, a little bedroom makeover can help set the stage for more (and better) sex.

According to Philadelphia-based ASSECT-certified sex therapist and clinical licensed social worker Melanie Greenberg, LCSW, CST, MEd, Founder of Pulling Threads Therapy, changing your surroundings isn't a guaranteed libido booster. “But having a bedroom that is decorated and designed to make you comfortable, free of worry, relaxed, and connected to your senses and partner may make it easier to get in the mood,” she says.

Rest assured, you don't need a huge budget to bring it to life. Whether you've got $20 or $20,000 to spare, there's a way to make your bedroom a little sexier. Here are nine expert-approved places to start.

You might think a bedroom designed with sleep in mind and one designed for, well, doing it are two different beasts. But actually they are the same side of the coin: “If your bedroom doesn't help you sleep, you're probably not going to have much energy left for anything else,” Erica Corpening, co-founder and designer of Hidden Honey Homes, an adult-only boutique retreat emporium, tells Cosmopolitan.

On a physiological level, skimping on Zzz's can disrupt our endocrine system, potentially interfering with the hormones that support sexual functioning in the first place. That's why before you start shopping for silk sheets and sex swings, Corpening suggests that when designing a sex-friendly bedroom, “start with sleep.”

First and foremost, that means getting a comfortable mattress—whatever that means to you. In particular, however, take a beat to consider your preferred squishiness, how much the mattress squeaks when you move, and the weight limit. After all, if you can't get comfortable lying still, odds are you won't enjoy getting busy in missionary.

It also means considering the bed height you enjoy most. “A low platform frame can make the room feel grounded, relaxed, and a little more lounge-like,” says Corpening. It can also make getting into floor-based sex positions a whole lot easier. But it might not be accessible for people with hip or knee issues, or anyone who has difficulty getting up from low surfaces. On the flip side, a higher bed frame can give your room a more elevated, hotel-suite feel, while also making certain standing sex positions easier to pull off.

The lowest-cost suggestion on the list doubles as the most essential: Tidy up. “One of the sexiest things you can add to a bedroom is space,” says Corpening.

The debris of life (i.e., burp cloths, bills, yesterday's boxers) can keep your mind focused on everything on your to-do list—a serious turn-off (or sexual brake) for anyone whose desire is easily derailed by distraction. And without all those visual reminders of your responsibilities, your brain might finally have the bandwidth for something a little less responsible.

“So before you buy anything, clear off the nightstands and rescue the corner chair that's been holding last week's laundry,” says Corpening. And don't underestimate the wonders that under-the-bed storage, vacuum-sealed storage bags, and drawer organizers—all of which are just an Amazon order or Target run away—can do for your daily detritus (and, by extension, your sex life).

Materially speaking, what do you need on hand before, during, and after sex? Maybe you're a sensation seeker like yours truly and need a Wartenberg wheel, blindfold, or feather tickler to transition from your PG life to your X-rated one. Maybe you squirt, or are into golden showers, and like to lay down a water-resistant blanket before play. Or maybe you're a lube lover who would benefit from keeping a bottle within arm's reach rather than buried in the bathroom cabinet.

Whatever your pleasure preferences, take stock of your sex-specific supplies, then do what you can to make them as easy to access as possible.“Even small adjustments to a real setting can make a difference,” doctor of human sexuality Emily Nagoski, PhD, sex educator and author, writes in her 2024 book Come Together.

“It’s not super-sexy to have to get out of bed, dripping with fluids, and go into the bathroom to get a towel, especially on a cold New England night,” she writes. Her solution? Allocate a drawer, reachable from the bed, with towels dedicated to post-sex cleanup. “Some problems are just that easy to solve,” she writes.

Sex furniture, more or less, falls into two categories. First, there is the stuff that looks like, well, the kind of sex furniture that outfits a dungeon or BDSM playroom. I’m talking St. Andrew’s sex crosses, spanking benches, and sex horses. All of which, for the record, can be great bedroom makeover investments if they support your particular fantasies.

But there's also more discreet sex furniture, like sex pillows and wedges, which are primarily designed to support your bodies and make certain positions more comfortable. While unassuming, these seemingly simple additions can have a serious impact because “with better support, sex naturally improves,” Danny Griffin, the creative director of Liberator, tells Cosmopolitan.

“A firm, stable surface at the right angle takes the strain off knees, wrists, hips, and lower backs, so you can stay in a position long enough to actually enjoy it,” he says. Meanwhile, the few inches of lift provided by a pillow or wedge can significantly change how two bodies line up, he says, “which can mean deeper penetration or an easier path to the spot that matters, which is a big deal for couples with different heights or body types.”

Ultimately, whether you opt for a piece of furniture that screams sex dungeon or one that passes as a regular ‘ole cushion depends on your wants, needs, desires, and budget. To figure out what you should invest in, Corpening suggests, “Ask each other what you'd actually enjoy exploring, what sounds intriguing, playful, adventurous, or more comfortable. Then choose something that supports that experience.”

According to Giddens, another cheap change with a big payoff is a lighting revamp. “Bright light is inspection light, and it mostly puts you on display to yourself, which can make it hard to feel sensual,” she says. Bright lights have also been linked to feelings of unsafety, which is totally incompatible with good sex, she adds.

Low, warm light, on the other hand, can help create a sense of privacy and comfort, allowing you to “stop monitoring and start feeling,” she says. On top of that, warm lighting can soften skin texture and even out tone, she says, which may help you feel more confident in your birthday suit.

Your move: “Retire the overhead after dark, put two lamps low and close to the bed, and fill them with warm dimmable bulbs,” Giddens suggests. Specifically, look for bulbs rated 2700K or lower. “2200K gets close to candlelight,” she says.

And if you're going for more sex-party than spa-night vibes? “Swap one lamp to a pink or red bulb, or hang a small neon sign, and let that colored glow wash across one wall,” she suggests.

When choosing a color palette for your pleasure palace, take a cue from the bisexual Pride flag: “Pink is the sleeper pick,” Giddens tells Cosmopolitan. Associated with both calmness and intimacy, the hue can help create a more inviting atmosphere, she says. “It also sits in the same family as flushed cheeks and lips, so it tends to flatter skin,” she says.

“Red is the obvious answer, and it earns its reputation,” she says. On the walls, red can make a room feel smaller, “which in a bedroom reads as close and intimate,” she says.

For a moodier, more boudoir-like feel, consider deep jewel tones like plum and midnight navy, which make a room feel more enveloping, particularly when the lights are low, says Giddens. In fact, if you're tempted to go full black-walled for the ~drama~, she suggests using these colors instead. “They will give you the same moody depth with some warmth still in it, unless a little dungeon energy is exactly the goal,” she says.

Whatever shade you choose, don't commit based on how it looks at high noon. Instead, tape a few paint swatches beside your bed and see how they look under the lamps you actually use at night. “Warm bulbs can change paint a lot. Reds, pinks, and plums tend to get richer, while a cool blue or gray that looks crisp in daylight can go flat and muddy,” she says.

Before you rinse your brushes and fold the tarps, consider giving the ceiling a little love, too. “It's the move that almost nobody makes,” says Giddens. Yet “it's the surface you look at most when you're lying in bed, it's usually still builder white, and there's no furniture to work around, so a gallon of paint and a weekend afternoon gets you a completely different room.” Cheers to efficiency!

Of course, the color you use on the ceiling matters. “A ceiling bounces that color back down onto your skin,” she says. “My one hard rule is no green on the ceiling, because that same reflection casts green onto faces and bodies, which is about as unsexy as color gets.”

A saturated pink or a warm peach above the bed, on the other hand, can cast a warm, flattering glow on the naked bodies below. Red, meanwhile, is better left to the walls or smaller accents, as Giddens says it can feel heavy and intrusive overhead.

Good news: If the thought of spending your weekend in coveralls with a paint roller makes you want to crawl under the covers (to sleep), you can still join the color play. Same goes if your landlord has a strict No-Paint-Allowed policy. “If paint is off the table entirely, change your sheets,” suggests Giddens.

“Sheets are the biggest piece of color that sits right next to your skin, and fabric reflects color the same way a ceiling does,” she says. As such, she suggests picking them the way you'd choose a makeup shade. “Pink, peach, deep red, and plum bounce warmth back onto your face and body, while gray-green does the opposite.”

A well-placed sex mirror can turn an otherwise innocent situation downright indecent faster than a well-timed 'good girl' can make me forget my own name (just me?).

Sure, there's the tried-and-true mirror-above-the-bed approach. Though, word to the wise: Don't leave this one to any ol' Taskrabbit. A broken mirror might mean seven years of bad luck, but a broken mirror above your bed could mean a trip to the ER. So make sure yours is professionally and securely installed.

But you have other options, too. “If you place a mirror across from a chaise, or beside a doorway, play can spill into other parts of the space,” says Corpening. “You might catch a bare shoulder in your peripheral vision, or the two of you moving together in the glass, and then it's gone. It can heighten your awareness of your body and build anticipation without anyone saying a word,” she says.

Meanwhile, a mirror near a window can serve double duty. “If you hang it near a window, it bounces daylight around and makes the room feel open during the day,” says Corpening. A win for afternoon nookie fans, indeed.

The Audacious Southern Chef Teaching Angelenos to Shut Up and Listen

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Vinyl bars like West Hollywood’s Darling, from star chef Sean Brock, will help you to appreciate music, and food, and life, all over again.

In our increasingly soulless world of algorithmic playlists and AI-generated art, one of life’s great analog pleasures is listening to records with friends. A dusty old LP pre-breaks the ice for new connections, unlocks stories you haven’t heard from old ones, gives you the chance to get collectively lost in the music.

Vinyl night is such a vital human need, it’s a surprise there aren’t more third spaces built around satisfying it. Characteristically, Japan is miles ahead of America on this front. In the country’s countless kissa bars, bartenders mix drinks and spin records, and—I swear this is true—patrons keep their voices down and listen. My partner and I saw and heard it for ourselves on a trip to Japan last year: every major city has a few joints where the speakers are big, the tables have signs that say NO LOUD LAUGHING, and the customers obey. At a packed and reverently silent bar in Kyoto called PM Sounds, a few whiskeys deep and well into the AM, side one of Jeff Buckley’s Grace hit just right. “Did we really fly halfway around the world to sit and listen to an album we have at home,” I asked Ben on the walk back to the hotel. “Yes,” he replied, and we both agreed it was worth the trip.

Americans are famously not a quiet people, nor are we particularly good with being told what to do. Could kissa culture happen here? A few places in Los Angeles are rising to the challenge: The Gold Line in Highland Park has a hip crowd and shelves swelling with the massive record collection of owner Peanut Butter Wolf. Hollywood’s The Living Room is massive and meticulously acoustically treated, and it’s currently one of the tightest doors in town. But in West Hollywood, across the street from the metastasizing gay club The Abbey and steps away from the log cabin where the hip AA meetings happen, Southern super-chef Sean Brock just might have hit the sweet spot: his first California restaurant, Darling, has an eclectic menu, outstanding cocktails, and speakers that will make you feel like you’re right there in the recording studio. “Music brings people together, just like food,” Brock says. “I've been obsessed with listening bars for a while, and I've been trying to get people into them since 2023. The thrill of discovery is amazing, and I think the more AI the world gets, the more we're going to reach for this stuff to balance it out.”

Brock looks every inch both the 21st-century Generation X chef and DJ: horn-rimmed glasses, sleeve tattoos, ADHD energy. He’s the James Beard Award-winning chef behind a string of beloved Nashville restaurants past and present, including modern Southern standby Husk and two-in-one restaurants Audrey and June. He’s respected by foodies and the hoi polloi alike; ask anyone who did time in Nashville this century, and they’ll tell you a snack at the bar of a Sean Brock joint is the reward for a hard day’s work. When we first met, just before Darling opened, we bonded over his massive collection of 45s in the DJ booth that looks over the restaurant’s bar area. As a secular song from New Orleans preacher Bobby Powell faded out, he wanted to play me something. “Have you heard this?” It was a cover of the Mamas and the Papas’ “California Dreamin’” by a Jackson, Tennessee band called The Jacksonians. “This is all they ever did! They teased us with this and then never did another thing!” As it played, we paused, we nodded our heads. “This is the one. When people hear this, they stop talking.”

The DJ booth and its speaker cabinets were made custom for the space. “The key is the wood,” he said. “13-ply Baltic birch, which is impossible to get now.” Within the cabinets, massive Italian speakers, with Altec Lansing drivers upcycled from their first life in movie theaters.

When Brock is at Darling—generally on the weekdays if he’s not traveling—he plays records as the restaurant opens, takes over in the kitchen as the dinner rush starts, then hops back onto the 1s and 2s after the last dish has been served, going as long into the night as the city will let him. “Getting to play music for people after working in the kitchen all night is my reward now. For the longest time, it was getting a beer after work. Now it's just watching music bring people happiness.”

As a record collector and a chef, Brock is always thinking about the evolution of local flavor. “What I’m chasing is the knowledge of the sound and the rhythm of a city and a time,” he told me. Take Memphis in the early ‘70s, for example. “There was Willie Mitchell at Royal Studios with the Hi label doing Al Green and Anne Peebles, and you had Stax. And then there was this anger building because of the Vietnam War, people are upset, and you feel that in the music.” He grabbed a 7-inch from the pile, the gateway 45 that got him into record collecting: Blackrock’s “Yeah Yeah.” And I did feel it, because the band is a murderers’ row of ‘70s Memphis session musicians, but mostly because of those speakers. At the right volume and in the right seat, the T. Rex from Jurassic Park has nothing on Blackrock.

At its outset, Brock intended Darling to give him a new identity away from the Southern cuisine that made him famous. “I didn't want to come here and cook a bunch of stuff I'd already cooked,” he said. “And we'd been told LA wasn't interested in Southern cooking, that it would be too heavy or whatever. So we didn't cook any Southern dishes when we opened.” The next time I stopped in, once Darling had been up and running for a few months, that whole idea had been 86’d. “About three weeks in, everybody was like: Where are the grits, Sean?”

“Now this has turned into a place where people come to taste the flavors of the South that I've been working on for 20 years — all the seed saving, all the research on traditions and recipes and history. I had gotten a little bored with it honestly, but all of a sudden I have a brand new audience for whom it's completely new, and I’m completely reinvigorated.”

He was describing, almost exactly, the experience Ben and I had hearing Jeff Buckley in Japan. Whether it’s food or music, there’s something revelatory about paying close attention, even to something you think you know.

So that’s the food, and I can tell you from experience that the steak burger is worth the trip (and make that trip early; he only makes 24 a night). As for the music, Brock believes in the power of the listening bar with the zeal of the recent kissa convert. “I had a kind of life-changing moment in a listening bar around 2022,” he says. “I was sitting in the sweet spot — the tip of the triangle, equidistant from two gigantic Tannoys, and the sound became 3D. It floated. I stopped drinking nine years ago, but I thought they'd accidentally given me alcohol.” He got on Nashville Craigslist that very night, searched “Tannoy,” and sent a message to a seller, who wrote back “I ate in your restaurant last night.” It’s good to be the king.

Brock has seats in the bar area of Darling situated at the perfect distance and angle from the speakers to give customers an out of body experience if they want it. But like a legendary Southern chef distancing himself from grits, a listening bar in Los Angeles is a risky concept. In Japan, the locals and tourists know to keep quiet and focus on the music, but this is West Hollywood, where everybody wants to be the thing that is focused on. Where if people have a song selector they trust, it’s usually their Peloton instructor. As an Angeleno, I have to worry whether anyone will pipe down long enough to pay attention. “There's a difference between a night where you come to eat and drink and socialize while music is playing, and a night where we're saying: what we're playing is special and rare. I want to test that theory soon,” Brock told me. He’s been promoting more and more listening sessions: tributes to jazz record labels Strata-East and International Anthem, a vinyl party hosted by Sylvan Esso, Dolly Parton’s first twelve albums, in order, on those THX-tested speakers.

Even the untrained ear can hear it coming together. I swing through again to hear local legend and NTS radio host DJ Morse Code spin a set of ‘70s and ‘80s jazz fusion and R&B. The restaurant is busy and buzzing, there’s a nice crowd up front in the good speaker seats, at the top of the triangle. Some of the folks up front are quieting down to hear the music, even as more people streamed in, even as the new arrivals were striking their poses, even as we realize that Anderson.Paak is at the bar, that Malibu cult singer-songwriter Ned Doheny is at a table in front of the DJ booth, that Kate Pierson is in the restaurant.

Darling is heating up, and the flavors, the sound, and the rhythm are continuing to evolve. The chef and the city are both learning to listen.

Want to Turn Back the Clock? These 10 Diets May Help You Age Better

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

You’ve got way more options than just the Mediterranean diet.

When healthy aging is the goal, it makes sense to try everything you can to make it happen. That means doing your best to exercise regularly, keep up with doctor's appointments, and eat a well-balanced diet. You have lots of options when it comes to the last one—but it can be hard to know which of the many popular eating plans is right for you. Thankfully, new research has helped narrow the choices.

The study, which was published in Nature Communications, analyzed several different diets to see how they impacted biological aging. (Biological aging is the age of your cells, while your chronological age is how old you are.) Popular eating plans like the Mediterranean diet and the DASH (Dietary Approaches to Stop Hypertension) diet made the cut, of course, but several lesser-known diets did too. Here’s what to know.

Meet the experts: Jessica Cording, RD, author of The Little Book of Game-Changers; Keri Gans, RD, author of The Small Change Diet; and Sonya Angelone, RD, PhD, a former spokeswoman for the Academy of Nutrition and Dietetics and a San Francisco-based dietitian.

For the study, researchers analyzed data from 6,470 participants who were 30 and older. Researchers looked at some of the participants' biomarkers that correlated with biological age and compared that with information about their diets.

After analyzing the data, researchers found that healthier eating in general was linked with slower biological aging, no matter which diet people chose. Each specific eating plan offered an edge with certain perks, though. Case in point: The DASH diet (which is specifically meant to stop hypertension) had more cardiovascular benefits for biological aging, while the MIND diet (which combines the Mediterranean and DASH diets for brain health) offered cognitive perks. But researchers discovered that there were a lot of shared biological aging benefits between the different types of diets.

These are the 10 diets flagged as being good for biological aging:

All of these diets are slightly different, but there are a few common threads. "They generally emphasize fruits, vegetables, whole grains, and other nutrient-dense foods," says Keri Gans, RD, author of The Small Change Diet.

The diets also stress the importance of minimally processed foods, while limiting refined carbs, added sugars, and processed meats, according to Sonya Angelone, RD, PhD, a former spokeswoman for the Academy of Nutrition and Dietetics and a San Francisco-based dietitian. "The recommended foods provide fiber, antioxidants, polyphenols, vitamins, minerals, and healthy fats that support metabolic health and help protect cells from oxidative stress and chronic inflammation," she says. Oxidative stress and chronic inflammation are both linked to a higher risk of disease, Angelone points out.

These diets also support the heart, brain, and digestive systems, says Jessica Cording, RD, author of The Little Book of Game-Changers. "All of these things factor into the aging process and somebody's quality of life as they age," she says. "When you're eating to support these different organ systems, you support your longevity and lower your risk of disease."

There are a few things to take away from the findings, according to dietitians. One is that there isn't a single eating plan that's superior to others when it comes to longevity. "It's the overall quality of what we eat consistently that matters most, rather than following a particular diet or avoiding individual foods," Gans says.

While there are plenty of diet plans out there that claim they can help you live longer, Angelone stresses this: "Healthy aging doesn’t have to include expensive foods, supplements, or restrictive diets."

Finally, the study underscores the power of nutrition in keeping you healthy. "Our genes are not our destiny," Angelone says. "We can change the expression of our genes—genomics—with nutrition and lifestyle habits."

Supporting healthy aging with the way you eat means giving your body the fuel it requires to function well, Angelone says. "Healthy aging isn’t about finding a perfect diet or miracle food or supplement," she says. "It is about consistently providing foods that supply nutrients needed by our bodies to function optimally, repair cellular damage, and protect themselves. This will impact how we age."
