James Harden admits to being ‘James Harden’

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Thirty-two points on 14 shots in a much-needed Cleveland Cavaliers win over the New York Knicks was a good reminder of who James Harden is. And in case there was still any confusion, the former MVP made sure to clear everything up afterward.

"My name is James Harden. I used to score a lot of points," Harden told reporters Wednesday evening.

That clarification was needed based on how Harden's first 11 outings had gone. He entered Wednesday's game in New York averaging an uncharacteristic 15.3 points per game, shooting a dreadful 36.4% from the field. Harden has been a step slow and unable to create space off the dribble.

There's a downstream effect to Harden not being able to score. The passing lanes dry up when defenders don't have to help cut off his drives. This has left him as an inefficient scorer and inept playmaker at the start of the season.

Wednesday, Harden showed that he still has the talent to be a dynamic scoring guard. He got the outside shot working in the second quarter, and then expanded his game from there. Defenders had to overplay him on the perimeter, which allowed him to attack inside. He used that to his advantage to score nine points in the fourth quarter, which included two finishes inside the paint.

In short, he was the Harden we've seen wreck defenses for over a decade and a half, not the one that we saw in the first 11 games this season.

Anything bought from the links helps support Fear the Sword. You can buy the comfortable Cavs hoodie HERE. You can also shop all of Homage's Cavs gear HERE.

The Cavs would be a championship contender if they could guarantee performances like this from Harden in the playoffs.

Being able to score and playmake at this level would mean teams couldn't load up to stop Donovan Mitchell, and it would create easy scoring opportunities for Jarrett Allen and Evan Mobley inside. But you can't ensure this version of Harden on a nightly basis, or even for big games he gets up for. Age catches up to every player, especially a 37-year-old like Harden who has played nearly 50,000 minutes in his 18-year career.

Instead of counting on Harden to play as he did in his prime, he's trying to find other ways to adapt his game to his current situation.

"I just focus on myself, things I need to do, and try to get in really good shape and learn my teammates and learn how to play a different brand of basketball, because I'm not the same fast person that I was like five or six years ago," Harden said.

Harden is still playing his way into shape, admitting that he wasn't as young as he used to be, so he "can't just be ready" at the start of the season. It takes a "bit of time to get adjusted to the speed, the pace, all of the above." But even when he does get back up to speed, the explosiveness isn't there for him to be the guy he was with the Houston Rockets.

Instead, Harden will need to find different ways to impact winning in this new reality he finds himself in. If someone can make that adjustment, it's a player as skilled and as smart as Harden. That adjustment won't happen overnight. There will undoubtedly be more lows like we saw in the embarrassing loss to the Charlotte Hornets earlier this week, but the Cavs are more than willing to endure those games if it means Harden can eventually find his way. Because in the end, the Cavaliers' hopes and dreams for this season and the next two depend on Harden putting the pieces together.

"Now I still score points, but I'm finding other ways to impact the game as well," Harden said. "And I think that's what people probably don't understand."

Lions vs. Patriots preview, prediction: On Paper

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Detroit Lions and New England Patriots are in Germany for a big international matchup. The Patriots are a bit desperate for a win, sitting at 5-4 and clinging to the final playoff spot in the AFC. The Lions are a little more comfortable in the fifth spot of the NFC playoff race, but if they want to contend in a competitive NFC North, they need this game, too.

So which team has that statistical advantage and will come out on top Sunday morning? Let's dive into the numbers with our Week 10 Lions vs. Patriots On Paper preview and prediction.

Note: Stats may be a little lighter this week because one of my bigger resources—NFL Pro—is not available in Germany.

The Detroit Lions had their worst passing offense performance last week against a bad—but improving—Dolphins defense. Offensive line struggles were the lowlight of the performance, headlined by the "suspension" of left guard Christian Mahogany and inconsistent play of rookie right tackle Blake Miller. Mahogany is back in the lineup, and that should help the Lions' passing offense which has remained near a top-five unit for most of the season. They rank:

And despite the poor offensive performance, the Lions offensive line is still grading out well as a unit. Though they rank 17th in sack percentage (6.5), they're fifth in pressure percentage allowed (19.4%), second in PFF pass blocking grade (75.1), and seventh in pass block win rate (54%).

The Patriots pass defense started off strong, but has struggled as of late, even against some of the most medicore-to-bad passing offenses. For the season, they rank:

For comparison's sake, all of these rankings except for dropback EPA are below the Lions' pass defense. So whatever you think of Detroit's ability to defend the pass, the Patriots have been worse.

But much like Detroit's pass defense, the one place they thrive is pressuring the opposing quarterback. Thought they're just 19th in pressure percentage (22.4), they're sixth in sack rate (8.0%), 11th in pass rush win rate (52%), and second in PFF pass rush grade (82.8).

While EDGE Elijah Ponder leads the team with 7.5 sacks, it's really New England's interior pressure that causes havoc. Look out for Christian Barmore (78.1 PFF pass rush grade) and Milton Williams (20 pressures) to give Detroit's interior offensive line some issues.

But New England's second is very beatable. Christian Gonzalez is having a down year (50.4 PFF grade) and the Patriots will be down Carlton Davis.

Player to watch: Amon-Ra St. Brown. This isn't just a Germany thing, St. Brown should thrive against the Patriots' zone-heavy defense. Brown is the Lions' best weapon when it comes to recognizing the soft spot in zones, just like last week's eight-catch, 86-yard performance.

Advantage: Lions +2.5. The Patriots' pass rush does have a chance to disrupt Jared Goff like the Dolphins did last week, but for most of the day, the Lions quarterback should be picking apart this poor secondary.

The inconsistency of this rushing attack is still very frustrating to watch, but putting things into perspective, it's still a very decent run game in Detroit. For the season, they rank:

Negative plays remain a huge problem, with Detroit's 20% stuffed rate ranking 25th in the NFL. They are improving in short-yardage situations, though, climbing to 18th in power success rate.

Still, the offensive line—though still plagued by some mental mistakes—appear to be moving people. Detroit ranks fourth in adjusted line yards, eighth in run block win rate, and, as a whole, the Lions entire team is fourth in PFF's run blocking grade.

Detroit will face a formidable test this week against a run defense that has allowed 100 rushing yards just three times this entire season, and each of their last five opponents have been held at or below 4.0 yards per carry. Though last week's performance against the Packers' worst rushing attack in the league should provide at least some hope.

Interestingly, the Patriots run defense appears more efficient than the sum of their parts. They rank just 16th in adjusted line yards allowed, 16th in run stop win rate, and 21st in PFF run defense grade.

One thing they do thrive in, however, is limiting explosives. They've only allowed six rushing plays of 15+ yards (third fewest) and just a single run of 20+ yards (t-fewest). But they're just okay in short-yardage situations. They're 10th in power success rate allowed (59%), but sport just a 16% stuffed rate (23rd).

Player to watch: LB Christian Ellis. While the Patriots are not particularly strong up the middle in run defense, Ellis sports the eighth-best PFF grade against the run (82.3), providing a strong second level of defense. The Patriots' secondary is also active and strong against the run.

Advantage: Lions +0.5. Detroit hasn't been as explosive as expected running the football this year, but they have found some consistency running for gains of 5-10 yards, and that could be exactly what we see on Sunday.

Despite a horrible start to the year, Drake Maye has seemingly righted the ship—outside of last week's performance against a very solid Packers pass defense. For the entire year, the Patriots pass offense ranks:

Essentially, if the Patriots could stop turning the ball over, they'd be a top-five passing attack. But turnovers have been a serious problem. Maye's 11 interceptions leads the league and his 4.0% interception percentage is third-highest in the NFL. To sport that dishonor and still produce the 10th-highest dropback EPA is pretty crazy.

So what's causing the high interception rate? It's a little bit of everything. An injured offensive line that ranks 21st in pressure rate (23.6%) and 18th in sack rate (6.6%), a quarterback who is still young and figuring things out, and a receiving corps that lost Romeo Doubs for the season and hasn't had a lot of A.J. Brown available.

But, again, the Patriots passing attack remains pretty dang efficient despite those problems.

I'm still hesitant to believe in Detroit's defensive improvement through the air. Much of that appears based on simply playing a bunch of poor passing attacks, but the one aspect they have seriously improved on is explosive plays allowed. In the first four games, the Lions allowed 20 passing plays of 20 yards (most in NFL). In the last four games, the Lions have allowed 14 (still t-10th most). For the season, Detroit still only ranks:

The pass rush (eighth in sack rate, seventh in pressure rate) remains solid, despite an ever-decreasing blitz rate (26.3%, 18th).

Detroit will be without top cornerback D.J. Reed for at least another week, but Brian Branch's return has allowed them to have a little more flexibility in their coverages, and it has calmed some things down (a little).

Player to watch: Hunter Henry. While Henry is far from 100% as he deals with a foot injury, he remains the biggest threat to Detroit's defense. Henry is first on the team in receptions (32) and second in yards (361). Meanwhile, the Lions is 31st in DVOA vs. tight ends.

Advantage: Patriots +3. This is the matchup that could change the game. New England, in my opinion, have one of the best passing attacks as long as they can avoid turnovers. The Lions aren't particularly great at forcing turnovers, creating just eight on the season—only three teams have fewer. So I would expect a pretty strong day from Maye.

New England hasn't run the ball very consistently, although injuries have played a large part in that, particularly as of late. They have a fullback, a running back, and three offensive linemen on IR. Running back TreVeyon Henderson has already been ruled out this week and top running back Rhamondre Stevenson could miss this one, too. Guard Ben Brown has been declared out already, and his backup Walter Rouse is working his way through concussion protocol. Put it all together, and the Patriots' running game ranks:

That said, running back Corey Kiner has stepped in and provided a bit of a spark in the last two weeks, rushing for 93 yards on just 15 carries, bolstered by a couple of 20-yard runs. Additionally, they are great in short-yardage situations, converting 75% of power situations, ranking fifth in the NFL.

The Lions' run defense is really starting to come on in recent weeks, with one of their best performances coming last week against a sneaky-good Dolphins run game. Overall, though, the numbers remain pretty pedestrian. The Lions rank:

They're still being victimized by some early-season explosives given up, but that has settled down considerably as of late.

What has been true pretty much all season is that Detroit has clogged the middle of the field. Led by the strong play of defensive tackles Tyleik Williams and Alim McNeill, anything between the b-gaps has been out of business. Yet, oddly, the Lions rank dead last in power success rate allowed, ceding an 84% conversion rate. So don't expect many third-and-1 stops from Detroit on Sunday.

Player to watch: Malcolm Rodriguez. Rodriguez is quietly having a very strong season, and with Jack Campbell out for the foreseeable future, his role will continue to increase. Currently, he sports an 83.9 PFF run defense grade (seventh in NFL among LBs) and the third-highest run stop percentage (11.7).

Advantage: Lions +1. With all of the injuries, I just can't trust the Patriots' rushing attack. And given that the Lions finally seem to have some answers on defense, this should favor the Lions. The one aspect that could tilt things in New England's favor, though, is Maye. While he still prefers to throw when he has escaped the pocket, he still does have 219 rushing yards this season, fourth most among quarterbacks.

While On Paper got wins both straight up and against the spread (6-2 overall, 4-4 ATS), it may have been my least accurate column of the year. I overestimated the Lions' advantage on offense and underestimated their advantages on defense. I expected a shootout and got a 19-9 win, instead. Sure, I had no idea Mahogany was going to be out, but that doesn't justify how bad I missed on that one.

To be fair, no one really expected a defensive battle. And the closest prediction in the comment section wasn't all that close: a 24-16 prediction from The Proudest:

I don't have photoshop on the road with me in Germany, so as your prize, enjoy this picture of me with a humongous beer in Munich:

The Lions come out with a slight +1 advantage, but the edge in three of the four matchups. As is the case with a lot of games this year, the outcome could come down to that one disadvantage: the Lions' pass defense. Maye is an electric player with a ton of explosive play potential. Detroit has done better at limiting that as of late, but the only recent quarterback they've played who can consistently do that was Jordan Love. If Detroit can repeat their performance from that game, they'll be in good shape. If not, it could be a long flight home.

#PostPulpit mailbag: What’s going on with Christian Gonzalez?

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The New England Patriots have landed in Germany where they hold a pair of practices before taking on the Detroit Lions at 9:30 a.m. ET on Sunday.

So, let's get into this week's edition of the #PostPulpit Mailbag.

Like a lot of people, I was mightily disappointed (to say the least) in the recent performance of our resident highest-paid cornerback in the league. But I find myself wondering if he's the only one deserving blame. When he repeatedly lines up in such soft coverage, not pressing at the line of scrimmage — indeed, giving the receiver an obscenely huge cushion to release — that's not entirely HIS decision, right? Isn't that pre-snap alignment dictated by the coaches and their game plan, at least much of the time? How much of the problem do you think lies with coaching schemes that play away from Gonzalez's strengths? – BriGuy301

What happened to Gonzalez last game and why? Was it a one-off, or did the Packers figure something out that other teams might be able to emulate? – fdsafdsa

Gonzalez is certainly not blameless and needs to play better. However, he is also being asked to do a lot within a Patriots secondary that leans heavily on disguising coverages. While the All-Pro cornerback is at his best pressing receivers at the line of scrimmage and playing man coverage, he has been asked to handle a variety of responsibilities, including off-man coverage, inverted Cover 2 (where he is responsible for the half-field safety), playing the deep third in Cover 3 and more.

The question here is whether the Patriots have leaned too heavily into those disguises and reached a point where simplifying things would benefit the defense. New England has a talented secondary that is playing well below its capabilities as they rank dead last in the league in zone coverage EPA per dropback. Opposing offenses like the Bears and Packers have also appeared to be one step ahead with coverage beaters and using tools like quick snaps to catch the defense out of position and generate explosive plays.

At some point, the Patriots may need to prioritize letting their defensive backs play more straight up and to their strengths.

With all the pressure that Maye faces each game, and especially against teams like the Packers who blitz a lot (46.5% of his drop backs against Green Bay), why do we not see more hot reads or short patterns? It looks like the Patriots rely predominantly on mid- to long-range routes, which take more time and require the Offensive Line to hold up longer. I would think that Pop or Chism – who both have short-area quickness – would be great at those routes, especially given that they seem to run a lot of them during the preseason. So I'm curious if A) they're doing it but I just don't see it? And/or B) is there a good reason to not run quick slants and crossers and hot reads? – PatsFanFromNH

The Patriots' offense does feature sight adjustments and hot routes. Earlier this season, players credited opposing defenses for dropping defenders off the line of scrimmage to take away those routes. New England will need to find ways to counter those adjustments or develop other answers against the pressure packages that have repeatedly given the offense trouble.

In recent weeks, both the Bears and Packers have found success attacking the Patriots' offensive line with overload pressures, sending two defenders off the same side of the formation and dropping defenders off the line of scrimmage on the other side. In both games, New England struggled to pick up those pressures early before settling down and finding answers as the game progressed.

Moving forward, the Patriots need to determine how they want to handle those situations out of the gate, whether that means identifying the right protection calls before the snap or trusting Maye and his receivers to make plays after. Finding those answers earlier in games will be critical to avoid early deficits.

Brian, if the Patriots lose to the Lions to fall to 5-5 with 4 very difficult games left, is it time to switch gears and root for draft position? It's been a tough year full of injuries. That happens in football, and the draft is supposed to be a good one. On the other hand, if they pull off the upset in Germany, it's full speed ahead going for the playoffs, with a bye week plus the Chargers game to get healthy. – New England Guy

Sunday's game against the Lions feels like an inflection point on the season, so it will be interesting to see which Patriots team shows up in Germany.

As for the playoffs, 10 wins has historically been a good benchmark for securing a postseason spot. If the Patriots take care of business against the winless Chargers, Jets and Dolphins, they would need just two more wins from their remaining matchups against the Lions, Vikings, Chiefs, Bills and Broncos to reach that mark.

And looking at the current AFC landscape, the threshold could be closer to nine wins. There appears to be a relatively clear-cut top seven in the conference unless the Houston Texans catch fire during the second half of the season.

Why didn't the pats trade for a nose tackle, 3rd safety, or/and a 3rd outside cornerback? Any one, two or all 3 of these positions were a must have to right the ship! – sjamesbossports

After investing significant draft capital in A.J. Brown and looking to avoid taking on a major financial commitment, the Patriots were never likely to make a significant addition at the trade deadline. You could certainly make the argument for adding defensive depth in exchange for late-round draft capital, but the lack of movement at those positions around the league suggests there wasn't much of a market at those spots.

A question more long term :what are your thoughts about next year patriots cap big moves renegotiation with major players (or potential cut or trade) to absorb anticipated Maye super contract ? – 7i165

Who do you think will be the cap casualties next year? Onwenu, Barmore, Davis, Hunter, Byard, DeVitto, Moses, Landry? I know they are a veteran core, but almost all of them don't produce to what they are being paid at, so we might need to cut bait. How much would we gain from trading or letting them walk? – Maxxwell

The Patriots did not sell at the deadline as they still can be a competitve team especially as they get healthier on offense. It will be a different conversation this offseason as there is definitely an argument for the team getting younger and adding draft capital.

Among the names mentioned, I would imagine the team moves on from Christian Barmore, Harold Landry, and potentially Carlton Davis as the veterans have no guaranteed money remaining. Byard's play has not been worthy of a second contract while the guess would be Moses retires. Henry was extended this offseason while there's no rush to move off DeVito. All the moves would free up a substantial amount of salary cap space.

That's all for this week's #PostPulpit mailbag. If you have questions you'd liked to be answered next week, submit them online in our weekly submission post on the Feed. Make sure to follow @iambrianhines and @PatsPulpit as well.

RedBird lancia 'MSE'. C'entrano Milan, NBA, nuovo stadio e non solo: di cosa si tratta

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

NEW YORK e MILANO – 13 novembre 2026 – RedBird Capital Partners (“RedBird”) ha lanciato oggi Milan Sports & Entertainment (“MSE”), holding che riunirà gli investimenti di RedBird a Milano nei settori dello sport, delle infrastrutture e dell'intrattenimento. La nuova società organizzerà singoli asset in un'unica piattaforma multisport integrata, con una gestione centralizzata, competenze condivise e partnership coordinate tra le diverse attività.

Capitale finanziaria e commerciale d'Italia e centro di riferimento globale per la moda, il design e la cultura, Milano rappresenta il punto di convergenza tra settori che stanno ridefinendo il futuro dello sport, dell'intrattenimento e degli eventi dal vivo. In un contesto europeo caratterizzato da nuovi investimenti privati, dallo sviluppo delle infrastrutture e dalla trasformazione dell'industria sportiva, Milano ha le caratteristiche e le dimensioni per affermarsi come uno dei mercati dello sport e dell'intrattenimento più influenti al mondo. MSE intende cogliere le opportunità offerte dall'incontro tra questi settori, riunendo realtà sportive di livello mondiale, infrastrutture, live entertainment ed esperienze culturali in una piattaforma integrata, saldamente radicata a Milano ma con una proiezione e ambizioni globali. Ma soprattutto, MSE rappresenta l'investimento e l’impegno di lungo periodo di RedBird a Milano e il suo impegno per il futuro della città.

Al centro di MSE c'è AC Milan, il secondo club più titolato nella storia della Champions League, con sette titoli europei, 19 scudetti e una comunità globale di oltre 650 milioni di tifosi rossoneri. AC Milan è alla base delle ambizioni di MSE: un'istituzione sportiva iconica, con un brand di grande forza internazionale e un legame profondo con la città di cui porta il nome.

Un elemento chiave di questa visione è il Generali Stadium, la futura casa di AC Milan, annunciato di recente: un nuovo stadio da 71.500 posti, progettato da Foster + Partners e MANICA. Lo storico accordo di naming rights con Generali, il più grande nella storia dello sport, unisce due dei marchi più iconici d'Italia – AC Milan e Generali – in una partnership che coniuga la loro ricca eredità italiana e l'impegno condiviso per il futuro di Milano. Più di uno stadio di calcio, il Generali Stadium sarà un polo all'avanguardia, attivo tutto l'anno, dedicato allo sport, ai concerti, all'intrattenimento dal vivo, all'hospitality e al retail.

Oltre al progetto del nuovo stadio, MSE intende sviluppare un più ampio ecosistema di attività sportive e d'intrattenimento a Milano. I piani comprendono la costituzione di un business nella gestione di stadi e arene per occuparsi dei servizi di ristorazione e vendita, del merchandising e delle suite premium; la valutazione dell'opportunità di detenere una franchigia con sede a Milano nell'ambito del progetto di una lega NBA Europe; lo sviluppo di una nuova destinazione per lo sport e l'intrattenimento dal vivo a San Donato; e la ricerca di opportunità per rafforzare il ruolo di Milano nel tennis internazionale.

RedBird ha una consolidata esperienza nella creazione e nello sviluppo di aziende nei settori dello sport, dei media e dell'hospitality (Legends Hospitality, On Location, YES Network, EverPass). MSE metterà a frutto le competenze di RedBird per costruire una piattaforma il cui valore complessivo, le opportunità commerciali e l'influenza globale vadano ben oltre quelli delle singole realtà che la compongono.

Gerry Cardinale, Founder e Managing Partner di RedBird Capital, ha dichiarato: “Negli ultimi 25 anni abbiamo lavorato al fianco dei rights holder nei settori dello sport e dei media per creare imprese innovative che hanno trasformato i rispettivi settori. Questa evoluzione si fonda su un profondo rispetto per il patrimonio di proprietà intellettuale alla base di queste realtà, su una partnership di lungo periodo con i titolari dei diritti e sull'ambizione di valorizzare appieno il potenziale di queste squadre e leghe, dentro e fuori dal campo. Milan Sports & Entertainment rappresenta il risultato di questo percorso e del lavoro portato avanti da quando, quattro anni fa, abbiamo investito per la prima volta in AC Milan. Diventare proprietari di un club ci ha fatto comprendere ancora più a fondo ciò che crea valore nel lungo periodo: vincere, essere al servizio dei tifosi e investire nelle comunità che danno significato a queste istituzioni. Oggi applichiamo questa esperienza su una scala diversa: non a un solo club, a un solo sport o a un solo impianto, ma a un'intera città e a un portfolio che comprende sport, lifestyle e intrattenimento. Conosco Brett Yormark e lavoro con lui da 25 anni, e non riesco a immaginare un dirigente migliore nel settore dello sport internazionale per contribuire a realizzare questa strategia. Grazie a Brett, possiamo portare a Milano il meglio dello sport e dell'intrattenimento dal vivo americani, contribuendo a scrivere un nuovo capitolo nel percorso di crescita e affermazione internazionale della città. Con il Milan come punto di riferimento, che sarà sempre la forza trainante di tutte le nostre iniziative, intendiamo costruire una piattaforma dello sport e dell'intrattenimento di livello mondiale, capace di plasmare il futuro di Milano e dello sport europeo e di riportarlo ai vertici della scena globale”.

Contestualmente alla costituzione di MSE, RedBird sta formando il leadership team della nuova holding e ha annunciato la nomina di Brett Yormark ad Amministratore Delegato. Riportando direttamente a Gerry Cardinale, Yormark guiderà lo sviluppo e l'attuazione della strategia di MSE, supervisionando le attività attualmente in portafoglio e valutando future opportunità di investimento.

Yormark porta con sé oltre trent'anni di esperienza nella creazione, nello sviluppo e nella guida di organizzazioni nei settori dello sport, dei media e dell'intrattenimento. Attualmente è Commissioner della Big 12 Conference, incarico che lascerà alla fine di gennaio 2027. Durante il suo mandato alla Big 12 ha:

•⁠  ⁠Guidato una fase di significativa espansione, crescita commerciale e trasformazione del brand •⁠  ⁠Concluso uno storico accordo per i diritti media con ESPN e Fox, valido fino alla stagione 2030-31 •⁠  ⁠Supervisionato l'espansione geografica della conference attraverso l'ingresso delle università dei Four Corners: University of Arizona (Wildcats), Arizona State University (Sun Devils), University of Colorado Boulder (Buffaloes) e University of Utah (Utes) •⁠  ⁠Portato nello sport universitario un approccio orientato all'intrattenimento, valorizzando i principali eventi della Big 12 e rafforzandone il brand attraverso iniziative innovative di marketing, partnership e storytelling •⁠  ⁠Istituito il primo Big 12 Business Advisory Board, riunendo esponenti di rilievo del mondo imprenditoriale per sostenere la strategia commerciale e la crescita di lungo periodo della conference

In precedenza, Yormark è stato Chief Executive Officer di Brooklyn Sports & Entertainment (“BSE”), dove ha supervisionato le attività dei Brooklyn Nets, franchigia NBA, e del Barclays Center. Durante il suo mandato ha:

•⁠  ⁠Svolto un ruolo centrale nel trasferimento della franchigia a Brooklyn e concluso nel 2007 un accordo ventennale per i naming rights dell'arena con Barclays, a un valore record •⁠  ⁠Affermato il Barclays Center come una delle principali destinazioni per l'intrattenimento dal vivo dopo l'apertura nel 2012, con circa 11.000 abbonamenti stagionali venduti e circa l'80% delle suite assegnate nella prima stagione. Nel primo anno, l'arena ha ospitato circa 200 eventi e accolto oltre 2,1 milioni di visitatori •⁠  ⁠Posizionato i Nets tra le principali squadre NBA e come brand globale, portandoli dal 31° al 4° posto nelle vendite di merchandising NBA e dal 27° al 5° posto nelle vendite di biglietti •⁠  ⁠Ampliato il portafoglio di impianti di BSE, guidando la ristrutturazione, la riapertura e la successiva gestione di NYCB LIVE, del Brooklyn Paramount e dello storico Webster Hall di Manhattan •⁠  ⁠Supervisionato tutte le attività del Barclays Center e di NYCB LIVE, tra cui gestione operativa, programmazione degli eventi, vendite e marketing

Brett Yormark ha dichiarato: “È un onore e un privilegio assumere questo incarico in una città con la storia e la passione per lo sport di Milano. Sono consapevole della responsabilità che comporta guidare un'organizzazione legata a istituzioni che significano così tanto per i loro tifosi e per le comunità di riferimento. Nel corso della mia carriera ho contribuito all'evoluzione delle organizzazioni sportive, ad ampliarne la portata e a creare nuove opportunità. Non vedo l'ora di mettere a frutto questa esperienza per realizzare la visione di RedBird per MSE. Sono entusiasta di lavorare al fianco di Gerry, di RedBird e dei nostri colleghi a Milano per trasformare questa visione in risultati concreti e duraturi”.

Man City have 'certain' Gianluigi Donnarumma exit route, hurdle must be overcome - Expert

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Manchester City could potentially offload struggling goalkeeper Gianluigi Donnarumma to the Saudi Pro League next summer.

That is according to former Everton chief executive Keith Wyness, speaking exclusively to Football Insider, who believes that the Citizens' options are limited in terms of where they can move Donnarumma.

The 27-year-old has faced scrutiny for his performances of late, with his current 65.8 per cent save percentage standing him 13th in the ranking of Premier League starting goalkeepers.

Wyness believes that the Saudi Pro League could provide a genuine option for City to cash in on the Italian, whose wages are likely to pose an issue for several European clubs.

Donnarumma was widely regarded as one of the world's leading shot-stoppers when he arrived at the Etihad Stadium back in 2025, but is seeing his reputation damaged a little of late.

The former PSG ace is on a significant contract with the Citizens, but is not producing the goods for them at the moment.

Everton’s former chief Wyness – who served as CEO at Goodison Park between 2004 and 2009 and now runs a football consultancy advising elite clubs – believes that a move to Saudi Arabia could be an option for Donnarumma, but is clear that City would have to play the market carefully.

The Pro League recently introduced a new budget cap which prevents clubs from spending over 75 per cent of their revenue on their first-team squad.

Speaking on the latest edition of Football Insider's Inside Track podcast, Wyness explained how that has created a hurdle for City to overcome with Donnarumma.

"Yeah, I do (think he could be sold). I mean, if this would have been just a year ago, I'd have said the Saudi market would probably have taken him up," he said.

"But, you know, the budget restrictions may be starting to bite down there slightly. So it's not quite the option for clubs that it once was.

"Although, in Donnarumma's case, I still think it could be one option, certainly."

Former Man City financial advisor Stefan Borson recently told Football Insider that Donnarumma is becoming a "very expensive problem" for his former club.

The football finance expert believes that Enzo Maresca and his staff simply cannot continue to accept the gloveman's mistakes longt-

Borson believes that a return to Italy is the clear leading option for Donnarumma in terms of an escape route, but pointed out that he would likely need to take a sizeable pay cut.

The shot-stopper is becoming a big issue for the Citizens, especially as their 114 financial charges continue to loom over them.

Nottingham Forest could make Zach Abbott U-turn after Southampton development - verdict

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Correspondent AUTHORITY Football journalist with experience at Reach PLC and PA Media; University of Central Lancashire graduate. FOCUS Fan-engagement content and match analysis across the Premier League and Championship. THE INSIGHT Oliver utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified reporting and fan-first content. He provides the human stories behind the results to ensure fans get the full picture as it happens.

Nottingham Forest may decide to keep Zach Abbott on loan at Southampton as more minutes beckon for him in a promotion-chasing side.

Oliver Glasner saw fit to allow Abbott out on loan in the summer, as he joined Southampton for the rest of the campaign on Deadline Day.

The 20-year-old defender signed a new long-term contract extension with his boyhood club upon agreeing to the loan move, proving just how highly rated he is at the City Ground.

His time at St Mary's has certainly not gone to plan so far, with just two appearances in their bid to win promotion back to the Premier League.

Recent reports have claimed that Forest are ready to recall him in January, but they may now be handed an incentive not to as Abbott pushes to start regularly under Tonda Eckert.

Keven Schlotterbeck's long-term injury is set to open the door for Abbott to receive more game time, as he looks to nail down a spot in their main eleven in the absence of the German.

Abbott's short Forest career has shown promise, with several decent performances in their run to the Europa League semi-finals last season.

He played regularly under Glasner in pre-season, and so it has likely come as a surprise to the 20-year-old and those at Forest to see him utilised so sparingly at Southampton this season.

Abbott was forced to wait over a month for his Saints debut, then was hooked off at half-time against Norwich on 14 October and failed to appear in any of their next four games.

Schlotterbeck's early injury last time out against Preston saw Abbott introduced from the bench, and he impressed despite a shock 1-0 defeat.

New reports have since claimed that the commanding German centre-back faces around six weeks on the sidelines.

Abbott looks to be in pole position to take his place in the starting eleven, meaning Forest may not be forced to recall him after all, especially if he does well and nails down a spot in the side.

Abbott won all three of his attempted duels against Preston, completed 90 per cent of his passes and made four clearances in a stellar showing from the cold.

Eckert praised his young loanee after the clash, saying: "He was very positive. I think he's done very well. It was not an easy game to come into, especially with the amount of long balls he had to deal with, and I think he's done that well."

Abbott will surely be the man to take Schlotterbeck's place in the team after the international break, and that will be music to the ears of Forest coaches and chiefs who want him to progress and return a better player next summer.

TCL QM7L Mini-LED TV Review (2026)

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Are we entering the era of TV “dupes”? Because to the untrained eye, or even a reasonably trained one, it’s hard to distinguish TCL’s mini-LED-powered QM7L from a lower-end OLED. So when your brother-in-law plops down in front of your new TV to watch the Lions and says, “Wow, you splurged, eh?” you can just nod and give a wink. Dupe accomplished.

I know this because the QM7L arrived close on the heels of a new budget OLED, the Roku Pro Series, so I was able to compare the two side by side. Results: surprising. Although it has a potentially problematic design element and an AI that’s not exactly Claude-level sophisticated, the QM7L might just be the best midrange TV you can buy right now.

Available in sizes ranging from 55 inches (which I tested) to a mammoth 98 inches, the QM7L boasts a virtually borderless screen that stands on a pedestal instead of the usual feet. That’s a win if you own a small piece of furniture that might not accommodate the width of such feet, and I’d argue it’s more aesthetically pleasing too.

However, it does pose a problem: Because the pedestal base sticks out in front by a little over 4 inches, it doesn’t allow a soundbar to sit snug up to the screen. You could balance it on top of the base, but that looks awkward. And because it slopes down, the soundbar doesn’t even sit level.

What’s more, although the base has two height options, the taller one leaves the bottom of the TV just 2.25 inches off your TV stand. That may be enough clearance for ultra-compact soundbars like the Bose TV Speaker, but something like the Sonos Arc, which measures 3 inches high, ends up blocking a sliver of the screen.

The pedestal is also kind of a pain to assemble, with little help from the tiny diagrams in TCL’s otherwise decent setup guide. There are eight small screws used to connect the riser to the base, four more to attach it to the TV, and a bit of confusion over which of 10 mounting holes you’re meant to use.

These are hardly dealbreakers. If you’re planning to wall-mount the TV, the base becomes a non-issue. Same if you’re skipping a soundbar altogether. Or maybe you’re willing to live with it sitting out in front a few inches.

Setting up the TV itself is largely a breeze, especially if you already have the Google Home app on your phone. Even though I’m an iPhone guy, I was able to scan an onscreen QR code and use the app to move quickly through the setup process. An account sign-in and Wi-Fi network selection later, I just had to wait on a software update before heading to the couch.

TCL’s unremarkable remote has sharp edges, the usual mishmash of Google TV buttons, and a pretty disorganized layout overall. On the plus side, it’s backlit and comes with six app-shortcut buttons.

One head-scratcher: The penultimate TV-setup screen notes that I can “customize the star button on the remote,” using it as a shortcut for this or that, which sounds great except that there is no star button.

Speaking of hallucinations (kidding!), Google Gemini comes baked into the Google TV UI, which is reasonably easy to navigate but not without a few annoyances. For example, the top menu includes a Live TV page—home to various FAST channels, simple enough—but also a TCL page that contains … other FAST channels.

As for Gemini, it’s nice to have but hardly essential. Just talk into the remote and you can ask for, say, movies about sharks, or learn who invented the Steadicam. The voice recognition is responsive, and the responses arrive quickly. Too bad it’s not hands-free; you have to press and hold a button on the remote to use it. Gemini also dishes up “watch now” options, and you can use it to create custom images and video clips, if you want. Unfortunately, while Gemini can accommodate basic TV commands (“Start Netflix,” “Mute the sound,” etc.), it can’t do things like open picture settings or toggling off motion-smoothing—a bummer because those commands would actually save you a little time. Instead, such requests turn into basic web searches, returning only information on how to access those settings yourself.

I was a bit disappointed with Google TV’s screensaver. It lets you draw from various sources, including an art gallery and your own photo library, but it doesn’t offer any “art mode” options like a mat overlay or automated brightness reduction. You also can’t adjust the amount of idle time before the screensaver kicks on. An art TV this is not.

Software quibbles notwithstanding, the QM7L is a joy to behold. Whatever you care to make of TCL's marketing lingo (SQD Mini-LED, the “S” stands for “Super”), the results can’t be denied: dazzling color and ocean-floor blacks that really do bring OLED to mind. In fact, I’d argue that this TV “pops” more than the aforementioned Roku OLED—not because the colors are more vibrant, but because there’s greater brightness behind them.

While watching Pixar’s Coco, for example, both TVs produced glorious hues, but my eye was more drawn to the TCL because the reds, greens, and blues just seemed to burst a bit more. (OLEDs, especially on the lower end, do tend to struggle with brightness, as detailed in my Roku Pro Series review.)

Even watching something like East of Eden on Netflix, with its decidedly muted color palette, it was the brightness that stood out; the QM7L revealed a bit more detail inside the Trask house. Interestingly, I also spotted a difference in the Netflix logo: On the TCL, it blazed a fire-engine red; the Roku version looked more subtle, almost orange in comparison.

Make no mistake, both TVs impressed me greatly. But seeing the two alongside each other, I liked the QM7L’s picture just a little better. I also watched sports and played Xbox games—both well served by the panel’s native 144-Hz refresh rate—and marveled at the smooth action and sharp lines.

Not everything was perfect, however. There were a few cases where contrast and brightness seemed a little lacking, like during an episode of the criminally underrated Halt and Catch Fire on the Roku Channel. Checking the settings, I found that the brightness had been dialed down quite a bit—but not by me.

The prime suspect? TCL’s AI-powered “smart picture optimization,” a common bit of marketing in modern TVs. If there was any behind-the-scenes intelligence at work here, I struggled to find it. Indeed, I had to manually adjust the brightness for that show.

Even more frustrating, where was motion-smoothing management? This scourge of modern television should be off by default. With the QM7L, it arrives in full ruinous glory—and the AI isn’t smart enough to disable it in situations where it doesn’t belong: namely, everything except sports and gaming. Luckily, it’s not buried 10 menus deep; you can toggle it off pretty easily. But this is one area where Roku proved superior, its Smart Picture Max adeptly adjusting motion-smoothing for most viewing.

As for sound, the QM7L is the rare TV that doesn’t straight-up suck, thanks to tiny, down-facing speakers buried inside the cabinet. This one has two Bang & Olufsen–branded cones you can actually see, and because they’re embedded in the backside, they face backward as well. That’s not ideal for a wall-mount situation, as the audio would be forced to kind of squirt out from the sides, but the speakers do sound pretty good when the TV is freestanding. TCL doesn’t specify speaker wattage, but I can attest that they get plenty loud, and do so without distortion. However, I don’t understand why they’re positioned just a few inches apart, as the cabinet appears to have room for much more separation. Consequently, the soundstage feels pretty narrow; there’s very little “surround” to be found here. Even with this above-average audio, you’re still going to want a soundbar—hence my complaints about the pedestal stand earlier.

But if my main complaints are a wide pedestal stand and so-so AI integrations, that's good news for the QM7L. The most important thing, picture quality, is stunning for the price. You buy a TV for your eyes, and with this one, they're in for a treat.

‘Definitely’: Birmingham City reporter delivers Chris Davies contract verdict amid impressive turnaround

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Birmingham City have won six of their last seven Championship games to surge to the top of the table after 15 matches. BCFC are in a fine position as the November international break continues, and the work of Chris Davies has drawn significant praise.

There have been doubts over his Blues future at times, especially during a disappointing 2025/26 season, but Davies has completed an impressive turnaround to secure his position. And, one man who believes the Welshman’s work should be rewarded with a new deal is Birmingham Live’s Alex Dicken.

When quizzed on Davies’ contract situation in a fan Q&A, Blues reporter Dicken wrote: “I think they should give it a few months to allow the relationship between John Murtough (Birmingham City’s new director of football) and Davies to develop – and get the transfer window done and dusted – but it’s definitely something that should be looked at before the end of the season.

“I think Chris Davies is the sort of manager who will want security and to know where he stands. It is remarkable what he’s done in the last six months when you consider he faced questions over his future at the club in March/April last year. He has shown huge development as a young manager.”

Davies started strongly as he led a strong Birmingham City side to a rampant League One title win. That was expected though, so the boss and his side rose to the Championship with a point to prove.

And, they failed to prove it. Last season, BCFC struggled for consistency and frustrated their supporters with football that was often drab and unimaginative. It led to calls for Davies to be replaced by a bigger name, but the hierarchy stuck by their boss.

There were some lingering doubts early this season, but the doubters have been silenced with a stunning surge to the summit of the Championship. Birmingham City play with much more speed and impetus in possession, as shown by the fact that they have scored 21 goals in the last 10 games. They’ve maintained defensive solidity too though, with Christoph Klarer and Phil Neumann starring.

Davies was criticised for not adapting or getting the best out of City’s attacking talents last season. He has done both of those things this term though, highlighting his own development and growth as a boss.

Davies dealt with the pressure of turning around Birmingham City’s fortunes. But, such is the ambition of the club that he must now take on the task of maintaining their impressive form.

BCFC have eyes on a rise to the Premier League with a new stadium on the horizon and dreams of a Jude Bellingham return remaining for Tom Wagner.

Davies’ importance in this cannot be overstated, and further security over his future would be richly deserved. His current contract runs until 2028, so entrusting him with the next stage of their project means a new contract will be required.

As Dicken argues though, perhaps it is best to wait until new director of football Murtough is settled. If a relationship can be built between the two, an agreement becomes even more logical.

Nvidia Employees Push Board to Bring Back Jensen Huang: Report

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nvidia Employees Push Board to Bring Back Jensen Huang: Report

Some Nvidia employees are calling on the company's board to bring back Jensen Huang, days after he resigned as CEO following reports of extramarital relationships, according to internal correspondence reviewed by Wired.

An internal "suggestion box" post, submitted by a longtime engineer prior to Huang's resignation, urged the board to take employee sentiment into account before acting, Wired reported. It has since drawn more than 1,600 comments, several of which asked the board to find a way to reinstate Huang. "If you believe in the mission, bring back THE boss," one anonymous commenter wrote.

The post also called on the board to share the findings of its investigation with staff and to formally seek their views on Huang continuing as CEO. One employee went further, sending a companywide email demanding transparency about Huang's exit and declaring: "I stand by Jensen."

In the hours following Huang's departure, some two dozen Nvidia employees posted supportive messages on LinkedIn, while Wired said it could not find any employee posts backing his departure. The engineer behind the suggestion box post declined to comment, and Nvidia and its new board chair, Harvey Jones, did not immediately respond to the outlet.

Huang resigned as Nvidia's president, CEO and a board member on Thursday, ending a 33-year tenure at the company he co-founded. Chief financial officer Colette Kress is serving as interim CEO, and Jones, a former CEO of Synopsys, became the board's first chair. Nvidia did not give a reason for the resignation.

Huang told staff on Sunday that he had been "unfaithful" to his wife, without naming anyone.

The New York Times had reported in October that surveillance of Alice Huang, also known as Huang Le, indicated that she had developed a "close relationship" with the Nvidia CEO. Per the Times, U.S. officials concluded she was a Chinese intelligence asset, while the Financial Times reported that a U.S. memo said the two had been romantically involved. China has denied that she is a spy.

Alice Huang was CEO and chair of Megaspeed, a Singapore-based Nvidia customer, and was investigated by Singapore police over possible false declarations relating to the company's server purchases, the country's Ministry of Home Affairs said on Thursday. She was not investigated for espionage, it said.

Police found insufficient evidence that she had committed any offenses, but concluded she had knowingly given them inaccurate information to downplay her role at Megaspeed, according to the ministry. She was issued a stern warning, left Singapore in February and was barred from returning. Investigations into Megaspeed and six other people are ongoing.

Alice Huang is now living in Beijing with the knowledge of security officials, Bloomberg reported, citing a person familiar with the matter. Attorney General Todd Blanche said on Thursday that the Justice Department had not yet concluded whether Huang was a spy, and that the FBI was investigating the reports, according to Reuters, which was unable to reach her for comment.

On Wednesday, the Times reported that Jensen Huang had also had a relationship with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia's vice president of health care, who reported directly to him. Powell has not commented.

On Thursday, Reuters reported, citing two people familiar with the matter, that Huang had an intimate relationship with a second Nvidia employee, whom it did not name. Nvidia declined to comment to Reuters, and Huang and a representative for the board did not respond. Huang has not commented on the reports since his Sunday email, according to Wired.

The calls inside Nvidia echo criticism of the board's decision from tech and crypto figures. Binance co-founder Changpeng "CZ" Zhao called it a mistake for Nvidia to let Huang resign, while Elon Musk said hiring him would be "an honor."

Huang still faces scrutiny in Washington. Congressional committees have asked him to sit for a closed-door interview on December 1 and to testify at a Senate hearing on December 3, as lawmakers examine Nvidia's chip exports to China.

He and Nvidia also face a proposed shareholder class action over his alleged ties to Alice Huang.

Nvidia reports quarterly earnings on Tuesday, the first under Kress.

Bitcoin falls below $70,000 as $870 million in bullish bets are liquidated

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Traders betting on higher Bitcoin prices faced nearly $870 million in forced closures over 24 hours as rate fears and NVIDIA uncertainty weighed on markets.

Bitcoin traded below $70,000 on Nov. 13 as heavy liquidations hit traders betting on higher prices, extending the crypto selloff against a backdrop of rate pressure and uncertainty at NVIDIA.

CoinGlass recorded $869.89 million in Bitcoin long liquidations over the last 24 hours, compared with $31 million in short liquidations. Over four hours, it recorded $559 million in long liquidations and $16 million in shorts.

By late Friday morning, UTC, Bitcoin was down to $69,224, with a rolling 24-hour low of $67,921.

The liquidation figures measure forced closures of Bitcoin positions tracked by the provider, a different measure from the change in Bitcoin's market capitalization.

Long positions benefit when prices rise. Their concentration in the liquidation figures is consistent with forced exits amplifying the decline as bullish bets come under pressure. The initial trigger remains unresolved, and position closures can involve different markets rather than a spot sale in every case.

The liquidation imbalance points to pressure on bullish positioning alongside the broader concerns about technology stocks and interest rates. Forced exits offer a plausible mechanism for amplifying the decline.

Bitcoin traded below $70,000 as long positions dominated forced closures. Select a rolling window to compare the imbalance.

Rolling 24 hours to about 10:52 UTC, Nov. 13: $869.89M in long liquidations and $31.18M in shorts.

Rolling four hours to about 10:52 UTC, Nov. 13: $559.87M in long liquidations and $16.22M in shorts. This window is inside the 24-hour window.

Long liquidations dominate both windows, consistent with forced exits amplifying a decline.

The previous US stock session ended lower, though the broad indexes' closing losses were modest. The S&P 500 fell 0.35% to 7,592 on Nov. 12, according to LSEG data carried by the Financial Times. eOption's closing recap put the Nasdaq's decline at 0.39% and the Russell 2000's at 1.66%.

The broker also described a stronger dollar and rising Treasury yields as investors anticipated tighter Federal Reserve policy. That places Bitcoin's weakness within a wider retreat from risk.

NVIDIA's leadership change remained part of that backdrop. The company announced on Nov. 12 that Jensen Huang had resigned as president, CEO and director, with Colette Kress becoming interim CEO while retaining her CFO role. Its SEC filing dates the board's acceptance of his resignation to Nov. 11, effective immediately.

Rate concerns also predated Friday's crypto decline. In a Nov. 10 speech, Fed Governor Christopher Waller said he leaned toward a 25-basis-point December increase even if October monthly inflation improved. He said a core CPI reading around 0.3% or higher could warrant considering 50 basis points. That was his individual policy view, rather than an FOMC commitment.

Microsoft was also still working to restore affected services on Friday. Its 09:57 UTC update, reproduced in Penn's Microsoft incident feed, said recovery remained variable. There is no direct link between the outage and Bitcoin's decline, however, the risk-off mentality that comes with global technological uncertainty is clearly affecting the broader market.

For Bitcoin, the immediate picture is heavy pressure on bullish positions alongside a difficult macro and technology backdrop. NVIDIA's results remain scheduled for Nov. 17, giving investors another opportunity to assess the company's outlook under interim leadership.

How ‘Red Ink’ Pulled Off a Single-Take, 1940s Vampire Newsroom Thriller

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After the L.A. fires derailed their original debut feature, directors Benjamin J. Murray and Michael Christensen made a "monumental pivot" to a low-budget period movie shot as a oner.

When filmmakers Benjamin J. Murray and Michael Christensen set up their New York indie production outfit Incline Studios, the plan was to launch their slate with Blood Bank, a darkly satirical vampire heist film set in an alternative timeline, about an underground gang of dissidents who stage a daring raid on the city’s blood reserve.

Christensen’s script for Blood Bank imagined a world where, years before, a vampire virus infected the population. The government used the infection as an excuse to discriminate and scapegoat minority communities. The ambitious feature was deep into pre-production when the L.A. fires hit. Related Stories Movies 'Women and War' Follows Four Ukrainians' Journeys Towards Healing From Trauma (Exclusive IDFA Trailer) Movies 'Pop Pop' Dives Into Radicalization and Male Fragility in the Digital Age. Its Creator Says It's "More Topical Every Day" - and Urges Us to Join Hands.

“Several people involved lost their homes,” Christensen recalls. “We knew we had to shut the film down.”

Incline was already heavily invested, having spent significantly in upstate New York. The plan had been to shoot Blood Bank in Syracuse to take advantage of the state’s tax incentives. Rather than write off that investment, Murray and Christensen decided to make another movie there, only a lot cheaper and a lot faster. Their solution was to make a prequel, set in the same fictional world as Blood Bank, but with a couple of twists.

“It was a monumental pivot,” says Murray. “We knew we had to make the film in one location and for a fifth of the money and a fifth of the time [of Blood Bank]. But we wanted it to feel big. So we decided to make it a period film.”

They also took a page from Netflix hit Adolescence, deciding to shoot the film as a oner, unfolding in real time in a seemingly uninterrupted single take.

The result is Red Ink, which had its world premiere at the ToruńCamerimage festival this week. Co-directed by Murray and Christensen, from a script by Christensen and Murray serving as cinematographer, the film is a low-budget, noir-inspired newsroom thriller. With vampires.

Set over a single morning in November 1949, Red Ink unfolds inside the bustling headquarters of Verity Press, a New York Times-style newspaper. Billions actor Toby Leonard Moore stars as Bob Hoover, Verity‘s editor-in-chief, a celebrated war photographer whose Pulitzer-winning image of Nazi concentration camps helped sway public opinion at the end of World War II.

Now Hoover is about to break the biggest story of his career: A mysterious virus is sweeping the globe. Reports are emerging of infected people with a lust for blood and raw flesh, and bodies turning up with puncture marks on their necks.

With the paper’s deadline approaching, Hoover must decide how Verity will frame the story and, in turn, shape the national narrative. His publisher, Walter Nash (D.B. Sweeney), is under pressure from the politically ambitious Senator Conrad (Mike Doyle), who wants to exploit public fear to justify a government crackdown.

The eight-day shoot brought together an ensemble cast including Siobhan Fallon Hogan (Men in Black), Tommie Earl Jenkins (Wednesday), Eugene Cordero (Loki) and Oona Laurence (The Beguiled).

“We had three days prep and an eight day shoot,” says Christensen. “It was really a labor of love, for everyone involved.”

The filmmakers drew inspiration from classic thrillers of the period, with the 1952 Humphrey Bogart film Deadline USA, in which Bogart plays a newspaper editor fighting to save his publication, a particular touchstone.

For the technical execution, Murray turned to the same camera system used on Adolescence: the DJI Ronin 4D, which enables extended continuous camera movements across four axes, adding a vertical dimension to the traditional three-axis pan, tilt and roll of a gimbal. Although Red Ink plays as a single take, Murray assembled the final version from “around 13 shots of 8-10 minutes each.”

Red Ink wasn’t supposed to be Incline Studios’ first feature, but it serves as an appropriate calling card for the kind of movies the company wants to make: scrappy independent features that sit between IP-driven blockbusters and the darker, less accessible dramas that dominate the arthouse space. A post-credit sequence even sets up the world of Blood Bank, bringing the filmmakers back to the project that started it all.

Next on Incline’s slate is Engaged, an adventure comedy set in Malta that Murray compares in tone to Indiana Jones.

“We think there’s a gap in the independent space for movies of the kind we love,” says Murray, “fun, more mainstream films with original stories.”

“We want to break the mold, to bring validation back to the indie space,” adds Christensen. “I think with all the doom and gloom in the world, people are ready to feel hopeful again, at least at the movies.”

If you've secured a DMZ Blackmarket Locker key, here are all the locations at which you can use it

You'll need to get yourself to one of the multiple MW4 DMZ Blackmarket Locker locations if you've found yourself in possession of a key for one, as it's the only way in. In contrast, if you've found a locker and want the goods inside, you might be wondering where to find the keys. Either way, I've got you covered below.

There are currently three Blackmarket Locker locations that I know about, and they're all marked on the map above, though you can see them in more detail in my more general guide to all MW4 DMZ keys. All of them are on the western side of the map, with two in the prison area and one in Deadtown.

Each location has two lockers in it, so if you've got two or more keys, you could take two with you each time to ensure you get a good look at everything on offer - just remember you can still only try and exfil with two MW4 guns. Alternatively, you could choose to just take one key with you and make the most of your weapon slots. I'd recommend taking a backpack with a secure slot and keeping your key in there until you use it, just in case.

Even without a key, these locations are great to check out as you can still find weapons and other valuable items in the locker room.

You can get keys for the Blackmarket Lockers in a couple of ways, one of which being finding them randomly by looting the map. A more reliable way of obtaining Blackmarket Locker keys is via Survival Kits that are unlocked through DMZ career progress, orders, and mission, as well as via the Battle Pass and events.

You'll acquire these Survival Kits, specifically Recon Equipment Kits, at levels 19 and 46 of your DMZ career. Once you've got them, you need to head to the Survival Kits menu in the DMZ lobby area, as seen in the image above, to claim them, at which point the items appear in your Stash.

How to unlock Flounder and Sebastian in Disney Dreamlight Valley The Keepsake Sea

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

The collection of characters from The Little Mermaid is growing, with Ariel’s beloved fish friends Flounder and Sebastian joining the village in Disney Dreamlight Valley. The process for unlocking them looks vastly different from all other villagers, though, and can be tricky to navigate.

If you’re hoping to get every character available to find and meet around the valley, figuring out what needs to be done to recruit these two is essential. Here’s how to unlock Flounder and Sebastian in Disney Dreamlight Valley.

Flounder and Sebastian can be unlocked by purchasing either the Deluxe Edition or the Ultimate Edition of The Keepsake Sea in DDV. These two Adventure Companions are exclusive content locked behind these special variants of the Expansion Pass, which means you must buy one of the two to get them.

With either of these versions purchased, you’ll then be able to unlock Flounder and Sebastian in DDV. These two are likely locked behind a few quests, probably ones that require you to first unlock Ariel and progress through part of The Keepsake Sea’s storyline to learn about Adventure Companions. We’ll provide an update here on all additional unlock requirements once they’re available to tackle.

Read what’s behind the latest news in gaming (with a bit of snark) by subscribing to the Destructoid Checkpoint newsletter!

Flounder and Sebastian will very likely only ever be available as exclusive bonus content unlocked for purchasing either the Deluxe Edition or the Ultimate Edition of The Keepsake Sea DLC. Most other DLCs have had similar exclusivity systems for special game editions, like Sven as an exclusive mount with the Deluxe Edition of Wishblossom Ranch.

All items included in special editions have never been available in any other way. This system has been in place for years, so it’s unlikely to change in the future. This means that the only way to get Flounder and Sebastian in your valley is by buying one of the two DLC game versions that include them.

Flounder and Sebastian are Adventure Companions, not full-on villagers in DDV. This means they act as a hybrid between a Companion and a villager. These special Companions can be given gifts, have daily discussions, and come with their own set of rewards, but are not quite as built out as villagers.

New Game from Doom studio and New Wolfenstein Announced

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Xbox has great news for Doom and Wolfenstein fans

Xbox is celebrating its 25th anniversary this month, and it's been spoiling fans left and right. Xbox games like Minecraft, Microsoft Flight Simulator 2024, Gears E-Day, and more received celebratory freebies. Beyond the awesome free in-game items, players were lucky enough to get a few exciting announcements coming from Bethesda-owned studios, Id Software and MachineGames.

Xbox CEO Asha Sharma took to X this Thursday to discuss improvements coming to Xbox, but that's not all. Sharma promoted the launch of Slaughter mode, an endless combat mode for Doom: The Dark Ages. In this mode, gamers will fight through increasingly stronger hordes of randomly selected demons to get a higher score and collect Skulls that can be exchanged for unique rewards. After each wave, they must decide whether to continue and risk all their progress or give up and try again later.

The Slaughter Mode update for Doom: The Dark Ages is scheduled to be released on Monday, Nov. 16. It is free for players who own the Revelations DLC or those who got the premium edition or upgrade for Doom: The Dark Ages.

Doom: The Dark Ages developer Id Software responded to Sharma's post about the update and announced that its teams in Texas and Frankfurt are working on a new game. It seems like the mystery game is still in the early stages of development, as Id Software was not able to share much, but it did confirm that it will be directed by Hugo Martin, who helmed The Dark Ages.

That wasn't the only new game reveal that we got on Thursday. Sharma confirmed that Wolfenstein 3 is officially in production at MachineGames. This was expected, as last year, studio head Jerk Gustafsson confirmed that the Wolfenstein reboot was always envisioned as a trilogy. However, confirmation of development is still a huge leap from mere rumors and discussion.

Both game announcements were short on details, so it's likely that they're still in the early stages of development. Nevertheless, it's great news for fans who were looking forward to more games from these studios. For now, we will have to wait to learn more, but thankfully, we have Doom: The Dark Ages' new game mode to keep us busy.

Tesla Just Killed Its Cheapest All-Wheel-Drive Model Y

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Browse this and many other websites without banner ads, personalized tracking and video ads.

Getting an all-wheel-drive Tesla Model Y just got a lot more expensive after the American company removed the entry-level version of the crossover from its online configurator.

The Model Y All-Wheel Drive, which used to cost roughly $42,000, is nowhere to be found on the automaker’s website, which means the most affordable dual-motor Model Y is now the Premium AWD trim that starts at $51,630.

Introduced in February, the now-defunct Model Y AWD came hot on the heels of the budget-oriented Model 3 Standard and Model Y Standard that debuted in October 2025 as more affordable, rear-wheel-drive-only versions of the company’s best-selling EVs. When the bare-bones Model Y AWD was introduced, Tesla also dropped the “Standard” badge from its lineup, though the other trims are still called “Premium” and “Performance”.

Powered by the same battery as the single-motor Model Y RWD, the discontinued Model Y AWD had the shortest claimed range in the Model Y lineup, with an EPA-estimated 294 miles on a full charge—54 miles less than the most affordable rear-driven crossover in Tesla’s lineup currently.

With two electric motors providing motivation, the dropped Model Y AWD could sprint from zero to 60 miles per hour in 4.6 seconds, 2.2 seconds faster than its single-motor counterpart, despite weighing roughly 200 pounds more.

The cheapest Model Y in Tesla’s lineup remains the basic RWD version, which starts at $41,630. Compared to the better-equipped and more expensive trims, the entry-level crossover comes with redesigned front and rear lights, passive shock absorbers, a lower 225-kilowatt maximum charging power, fewer speakers for the audio system, and seats trimmed in vegan leather and cloth. There’s no FM radio, no rear screen, no ventilated seats, and only the front seats are heated. Furthermore, while the glass roof is still installed, the base trim has a headliner that covers it entirely.

The discontinuation of the Tesla Model Y All-Wheel Drive is just the latest casualty in the company’s quest to slim down its passenger-vehicle lineup. Earlier this year, the Model S and Model X were axed, and their production line has been ripped out of the factory to make room for robots. More recently, Tesla CEO Elon Musk announced that the Cybertruck would be retired next year. Until that happens, the only trim remaining is the top Cyberbeast, which starts at $109,990.

There are no plans to replace the Model S and Model X, and the Cybercab two-seater will take over the Cybertruck’s floor space.

Who could England face in Nations League quarter-finals?

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Nations League quarter-finals, semi-finals and final format explained, with draw for last eight in November after groups decided; England have progressed to knockouts but can win group with victory over Spain in Madrid on Sunday

England have sealed their place in the Nations League quarter-finals after beating Croatia at Wembley - but who will they play in the last eight in March?

England are currently second in Nations League Group A3 behind Spain, trailing the world champions by three points.

Thomas Tuchel's side lost to Spain at Wembley at the start of this campaign and have their final match of the group stage in Madrid on Sunday.

Both sides are assured of a quarter-final place but a win for England will mean they finish top of the group.

That matters as the winners of the Nations League tier A groups are seeded for the quarter-final draw. They will play teams which have finished runners-up in other groups. They will also play the second leg of their quarter-final at home.

If England draw or lose against Spain, they will finish second and face a group winner in March.

Please use Chrome browser for a more accessible video player

After matches on November 12, the sides below are in contention for a quarter-final qualifying place in the three other tier A Nations League groups:

Group A4: Portugal, Denmark, Wales, Norway

The draw for the last eight will take place on November 20 at 11am.

The quarter-finals will be two-leg ties, with the seeded group winners playing the second match at home.

The matches will take place in the March international break, with the first legs between March 25-27 and the second legs between March 28-30.

The Nations League Finals will then be held between June 9 and June 13, 2027.

The tournament will feature two semi-finals, a third-place play-off and the final, with four nations competing to be crowned champions of Europe.

The four semi-finalists will be invited to make bids to host the semi-finals and final.

Beond Airlines Suspends Flights Again: Will They Resume In December?

📅 Nov 13, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Back in April 2026, we saw Beond Airlines suspend all scheduled operations for the summer, as the airline claimed it would resume flights as of October. Then in October, it announced it would return in December. Is that actually happening now, or what exactly is going on?

Beond Airlines launched operations in 2023, and it brands itself as the world’s first premium leisure airline. The company is headquartered in Dubai, but primarily operates flights to and from the Maldives. I reviewed the airline in 2024, and the onboard product was super pleasant. The airline currently has a fleet of two aircraft, including one A319 and one A321.

Anyway, in mid-April 2026, the company announced that it would suspend its scheduled operations for the entire summer. The airline promised to resume flights in October, for the winter season.

Then in October, the airline announced that it would suspend operations through November 30, 2026, meaning it will resume flights as of December 1, 2026. Interestingly, the airline has promised to provide a schedule update by November 12, 2026, but that date has now come and gone, and there was no update.

So I guess it remains to be seen if we actually see a resumption of flights as of December, or if the timeline will get pushed back again. The company does have a section on its travel updates page with FAQs, and one of the questions is “will the dates change again?” The answer is “we chose dates we are confident in,” but also notes that “if anything changes, you will hear from us first.”

Beond Airlines does have its “commitment” to customers when plans change, which includes the ability to change destinations or travel dates, receive a travel credit valid for 18 months, or to receive a full refund. A change of destinations or dates, plus a travel credit, isn’t worth a whole lot for an airline that keeps pushing back its relaunch.

Interestingly, but perhaps not surprisingly, earlier this year the airline deleted its customer promise webpage, which used to guarantee rebooking on another airline in business class in the event a flight is canceled, or else the airline would offer a free flight. That page is now gone, and there’s no mention of rebooking on another airline.

So the airline will “support you every step of the way,” unless you actually want to take the vacation you booked, in which case you’re out of luck (which is less than ideal, given that many Maldives properties have very strict cancelation policies).

While Beond Airlines’ business plan was questionable under the best of circumstances, the crisis of this year makes matters worse:

So I feel bad for Beond, and I’m not trying to do a “told you so, I was right” thing, because these are of course exceptional circumstances. The thing that made me suspicious about Beond for so long is that the airline would repeatedly make outlandish growth announcements, only to rarely follow through on them. That’s neither here nor there, though.

Keep in mind that since operations were initially suspended in April, we’ve seen some interesting developments:

My assumption is that Saudi Arabia is to some extent funding the Red Sea flights, given how the country is throwing money at any airline willing to fly there. However, one wonders if that’s enough money to make the math work, or if Beond even has the general funding right now to keep things operating.

It’s just all so strange, because it feels like half of the announcements from Beond Airlines are really targeting prospective investors, constantly promoting how it’s going to open new subsidiaries all over the world, launch dozens of new routes, etc.

As I said in April, I didn’t think it was a sure bet that flights would resume in October. Now we’ll see if flights actually resume in December.

In the spring of 2026, all-business class airline Beond suspended scheduled operations through October 2026. That timeline has come and gone, and now the airline claims it’ll resume operations as of December 2026. Over the summer, we learned that the airline wasn’t paying many staff for months, and the airline is now pivoting to operating service via Saudi Arabia’s Red Sea Airport.

We’ll see what happens in the coming weeks. Presumably the Red Sea service gets the airline some money from the Saudi government, but it remains to be seen if that’s enough to keep this operation afloat.

It’s understandable that the current situation is really challenging, between the price of jet fuel, plus overall sentiment about the Middle East. However, I have to imagine the airline didn’t have great financial health before this whole mess started, and suspending flights in some ways only makes things even worse.

How do you see this playing out for Beond?
